# Tooling recipes

The planned recipes file (`docs/README.md`). The recipes `docs/MEMORY-DIET.md` still owes it stay owed. Machine
paths are set by environment variable (`tools/harness/lib/paths.py`).

## The tools every item reuses

`AGENTS.md` rule 21: read this before writing a harness script, use what is here, and add a row for anything built
that a later item could use. The files are in `tools/harness/` (its `README.md` says how to set it up): `lib/` is
shared by every item, `original/` drives the original, `dotnet/` holds the C# readers. A `q<N>/` folder in the
work folder (`OPENTPW_HARNESS_WORK`) is one item's own evidence, not a place to look for tools. Each file's
docstring is its manual. A row whose file is not in `tools/harness/` (`perf/`, `handoff/`, `fork-review-2/`,
`autorun/`, `strdump`, `nodenames/`, `meshhash.py`, `gen_addresses.py`, `lib/testme.objdump`) is a maintainer's tool kept outside the repo.

| Tool | Use it for |
|---|---|
| `lib/parkrun.py` | The scaffold of every save-and-load confirm run (it sets `OPENTPW_LOG_DIR` to the run's `OUT` unless the script has, so the game's `console.log` pair lands in the run's folder and never rotates the game folder's own; Q647): a private game folder, `enter()`, `save()`, `load( row )` (this game's Load list runs by name, so a file copied to `WROTE` is not row 1 beside `autosave`: Q688 loaded it as row 2), `reply()` (fenced: a unique unknown command follows each one, so every line before its answer is the command's, however slow the frames; it waits at least `secs`, at most `limit`, then stops loudly), `own_file( path )` (a file the run makes in the private folder: `done()` removes it, or the next start if the run was killed), `gaps( pattern )`, `shot()`, `predict()` and `said()`, `done()`; `OFFSCREEN = True` for a run beside the desktop's, on `:78` or `OFFSCREEN_DISPLAY` (a two-lane run's lane B takes `:79` and `GAME_FOLDER = "~/.cache/tpw-harnesses/lib/game-off-B"`); `GAME_FOLDER = "<path>"` for a game folder of the script's own (`lib/gamefolder.py`); `AUDIO_FILE = "<path>"` writes the game's own mix to a file, nothing to the speakers; `LEDGER = "<path>"` keeps the ledger of made files there, not in the private game folder, which a read-only one (`gamefolder.fresh`) refuses; `original_view( x, y )` for a saved camera the original's frame will show the point in. The game runs in a systemd scope of its own capped at `OPENTPW_GAME_MEMMAX` (8G by default, `off` for none; `gamedrive.py`), and `done()` prints its `game memory peak`: since Q954 the stock park peaks near 0.9 GB off-screen and Alexah's Full Simulation park near 1 GB (the cap is a safety net now, and a run that nears it is a regression) |
| `lib/gamefolder.py` | A private game folder whose data differs from the real game's in the files named: `make( dest, { "data/Advisor/Advisor.sam": change } )` links everything else and writes a changed copy of each file (`once( old, new )` is a change that fails unless `old` is there once); hand the folder to `parkrun.py` as `GAME_FOLDER`, on the desktop or off it. For a balance key changed in this game without touching `/home/alex/Games/TPWorld` (Q338). **`gamefolder.py lock DEST`** (`lock( DEST )`, then `unlock( DEST )` before the folder is removed) makes one whose `save/` is read-only at every level and whose first player has no `restart.INTS` (Q982). Since Q984 the game then keeps its saves in the player's data folder, so set `XDG_DATA_HOME` to a folder of the run's own first (read-only too for Q982's boxes); `lock` and `fresh` refuse when it is unset, unless `GAMEFOLDER_ALLOW_REAL_HOME=1`. **`gamefolder.py fresh DEST`** (`fresh( DEST )`, then `unfresh( DEST )`) makes one that cannot be written and has no `save/` at all, a stock Program Files install (Q984; the game keeps its saves in the player's data folder then, so set `XDG_DATA_HOME` to a folder of the run's first, and `LEDGER` for `parkrun.py`) |
| `lib/seedfile.py <in> <out> <old> <new>` | A copy of a park file with its `mRandomSeed` written over (the one place in the inflated block holding `<old>`, hex; the stock park's and `q340/late30.TPWS`'s is `3f416bba`), the block packed again: a different draw at the original's summons (Q583: seed 1 makes the ferry there). The original's draw is its whole state, not the seed's first, so try seeds |
| `lib/kart.py [-v] <file>...` | Walks the track-rides module of park files |
| `lib/bumpertemplates.py [testme.exe]` | The track rides' 14 templates read from the executable's data (`0x764178`): each BumperType's duration, radius, `w`, first mesh, mesh count and performance, the words a ride's record is filled from and a park file keeps (Q858) |
| `lib/pk.py` | A park file taken apart and put back: `Park(path)` parses, `.write(path)` deflates the body behind the file's own preamble (Q241h) |
| `lib/wadcat.py` | A `.wad` read without the game: a Python port of `WadArchive.ReadArchive` and `Refpack.Decompress` |
| `lib/bindtables.py` | Every row of the original's six key tables (`FUN_0040cb80`: system, game, camera, cheat, coaster, shortcuts), read from `testme.exe`: action, key (`0x2600` an extended key's VK shifted, a lower-case letter a keypad VK), modifier, name and handler; then the letters bound and free with no modifier, for a key OpenTPW gives a job of its own (Q631) |
| `lib/mixmatch.py` | One sample measured in OpenTPW's own mix (`AUDIO_FILE`): `template( sdt, index )` decodes a bank's entry, `window( file, from, to )` cuts the capture between two sizes, `match()` is the best match and its least-squares gain, `steady()` the gain of a looped sample under other sounds, from 0.3 s pieces in 2 s steps, `pitched( t, pitch )` the template at a voice's drawn pitch in 96ths of an octave (a voice off pitch nought matches only so; Q612) |
| `lib/lobbyvoices.py OUT BIN [SECS]` | The lobby's voices with nobody playing, and whether given samples are in its mix (Q770): `voices` and `sound` polled every 0.6 s into `voices.txt`, two shots, then the count of polls naming `LOBBY_SAMPLES` (default global lobby effect 3, `global/sound/sSfxHD.sdt:0,1,2,3`, `ew_space_1` to `4`) and each one's `mixmatch.match` score in the mix beside the jungle themes the census names as the control (the first theme sits under the advisor's opening line and scores low, 0.09 to 0.50; the others heard at full gain score 0.65 to 0.94: read a later one). 2026-10-31, 60 s: the bed in, 41 and 42 of 99 polls and a best sample 0.53 and 0.55 (the 0.295 to 0.533 of the first); the bed out, 0 of 99 and 0.095 to 0.107. The original's side: `original/voices.py PID 60` (its live voice list) and `original/capmatch.py` on an `ORIG_ASOUND` capture, `original/themescan.py` as the control (`OPENTPW_GAME_PATH` set). Q770 |
| `lib/netfake.py` | A run's stand-ins for the network and the browser (Q941): `listener( "record" \| "hole" \| "closed" )` is a loopback proxy that notes each connection's first line, never answers, or refuses; `proxy_env( srv )` points the run's `HTTPS_PROXY` at it before the game starts; `browser_shim( OUT )` puts an `xdg-open` first on `PATH` that records its arguments in `OUT/opened.txt` (and drops the D-Bus session, so SDL's `OpenURL` cannot reach the desktop's portal): the game's call out is seen and nothing leaves the machine. `python3 netfake.py` checks itself. It counts connections, and .NET makes more than one when a proxy hangs up on it at once (16 for one request, `q941/runs/fake-final`). Q941 |
| `lib/framesweep.py` | exec'd after `parkrun.py`: `sweep( cmd, pat, secs, tag, trigger, step=0.1 )` sends the console `trigger` (`strike`, `bolt X Y`), then every `step` seconds grabs a frame and sends `cmd`, keeping the first reply line containing `pat`; saves `<tag>-NN.png` and answers `[(seconds, census line, frame)]`; `val( line, key )` reads `key=number`; `mask_of( samples, base, box, thr, after=0.05 )` answers the boolean mask and `lift( samples, base, box, thr, mask=None )` answers the size and the per-sample mean lift of the pixels the first sample lifted by more than `thr` grey levels over a pre-trigger `base`, inside `box` (a plain difference of a live scene carries hundreds of pixels of its own motion; the mask measures the thing alone: check its size). The seconds are wall clock and skid a frame or two behind the thing's own age (0.25 s seen on a loaded machine): keep every bound 0.1 s or more from a boundary. A whole-lobby mask is not usable: after `freeze` the ocean, the mascot's hands and the flyers move about 25,000 px over 15 grey levels in a second, bolt or none (Q1034). `stepsweep( cmd, pat, n, tag, trigger=None, steps=3 )`: the same on a PAUSED clock, a `step` of 1/60 s each, the age exact (Q1034, `q1034/confirm2.py`; its recipe is below). Q152, `q152/confirm.py`; the `near` (lobby) and `state` (park) censuses print the bolt's `flash` and `life` / `boltLife` |
| `lib/parkscene.py` | exec'd after `parkrun.py`: the one-line census readers (`var`, `thing_line`, `staff_line`, `bumper_line`, `field`), the Hot Pot scenes (`hot_pot`, `in_a_go`, `broken_pot`), an advisor tag's rect and the window point to click it from `tags`' reply (`tag_rect`, `tag_click`; Q415), and the pointer's: `pointer()` and `pick()`'s lines, `at( fx, fy )` a share of the window, `find_person( want )` the pointer stepped round the middle until `pick` names a person (a locator's camera keeps its person there), `ctrl( k )` (Q451), a person's pick circle in window pixels, `circle( thing )` and every drawn person's `circles()` (the console's `circle`, Q468; its middle to a tenth of a pixel since Q628), `wait_log( pattern )` for a game line that reaches run.log a moment late (Q468), `find_cell( (x, y), sx, sy )`, the window pixel near a start that `pick` answers with a named map cell, the hover's own (Q471, Q478), and a screen's own points: `layout_point( frame, rect, vx, vy )` a point of the 2048 x 1536 layout in window pixels through its frame control's place, `press_layout( ... )` a real click there, `drag( p0, p1, what, pressed=, held= )` a real drag between two window points (a slider's thumb; Q590), with `pressed()` run once the button is down and `held()` once it has moved, before the release (a census while a thumb is held; Q705), so a confirm script does not write them again. Its `at` and `field` are its own: a script that defines either after the exec breaks the helpers that use them |
| `lib/sellload.py <outdir> <bin> <park file> <thing> [camera]` | A park file loaded, one thing sold, the park saved and the saved file loaded (Q926): the file copied in as the private player's newest, Load row 1, `sell <thing>`, `pause`, `savepark`, Load row 2 (the saved file is the second name in the list; row 1 reloads the unsold one). Each of the three reads prints `scripts`' count with the sold thing's and every thing-0 script's lines, the first `bumpers` line, the Engine voices, the log's Car sounds lines and the `unimplemented` lines of SAVE_PARK, LOADED_ and TRACK_CAP, and takes a frame at `camera` (default `435 250 60 180`, a Hot Pot's site); the saved file is copied to `<outdir>/sellload-saved.TPWS`. Run it with the venv's python, `<bin>` the build's folder; a bug put back is a worktree of the commit with the line changed, built into its own folder, run the same way |
| `lib/boughtload.py <outdir> <bin> [camera [steps [sweeps a step]]]` | A Hot Pot bought and run in the stock jungle park, saved and the saved file Loaded (Q929): `hot_pot()`, `in_a_go()`, a read (`scripts` lines of the pot and thing 0, `bumpers`, the Engine lines of `voices`, the log's Car sounds count, the save and load gaps), `savepark`, Load row 1, the same read, then the paused park stepped (`steps` times, default 30, of `sweeps a step`, default 48: about 15 sweeps a game tick) with a `STEP` line each (tick, the pot's script word, its state, seated, Engine voices) and a frame (`3-stepped.png`, at the last step). The run stops as `VOID` (exit status 3, nothing saved) when the pot is not Running with somebody seated in 120 s. The 2-loaded read is at the load's own tick, before a sweep, so its Engine voices are 0 by design. The saved file is copied to `<outdir>/boughtload-saved.TPWS` (`lib/scriptrecords.py` reads it; `original/` is given it by the recipe below). Needs `OPENTPW_GAME_PATH` set. |
| `lib/kartload.py <outdir> <bin> [camera [steps [sweeps a step]]]` | A Dino Karts bought and run in the stock jungle park (`KIND=water` in the environment: a Splish Splash), saved and the saved file Loaded (Q939), the kart's twin of `boughtload.py`: `karts()` buys it, lays the loop by Q763's four clicks and, for the karts, a path from the street's spur (39,23) west to (32,23), the queue joined there and five guests sent; `RUNSECS` (default 40) seconds on, then the same reads (`scripts`, the ride's and every thing-0 line; the log's gaps), the save, the Load, the reads again and the steps. The karts' cars are not in `bumpers`, so there is no "in a go" to wait for. Each read also prints the ride script's `VAR_ONRIDE`, `VAR_LETMEON`, `VAR_SPACELEFT` and `VAR_RIDECLOSED` (`scriptvar`), the counted `BUMP at N` lines by word (Q883: the kart's script now runs past word 18, and a read of 6,000 `BUMP at 15` is the old bug) and one `peeps` line for each guest sent. The saved file is `<outdir>/kartload-saved.TPWS`. |
| `lib/scriptrecords.py [-s] <park file>...` | A park file's script module one line a record, `h HANDLE thing T pc PC child C parent P sound S dir D`, a count first (`records( path )` gives dicts): which records a sale, a purchase or a close left in a saved file, to put beside the console's `scripts` and `original/origscripts.py` (Q926; that prints `h pc thing child parent sound`, this `h thing pc child parent sound dir`, so compare by field, not by line). `-s`: each record's speed word and play rate (`+0xc0`, `+0xe4`), then each placed object's `mOperatingSpeed` beside its script's word (`objects( path )`; Q897) |
| `lib/closeload.py <outdir> <bin> <label> <park file> <thing> [camera]` | A ride closed after a load and saved (Q927): the file (a park saved with the ride OPEN and its companion child alive) copied in as the private Full Simulation player's newest, Load row 1 (the load starts the companion), `scriptvar <thing> VAR_RIDECLOSED 1` (the script's variable and nothing else: `ParkRideOperation.Close` also writes CanLoad 0 and raises the Closed hoarding, which a bought ride has had since the buy, so the ride is closed in its script only; a bought ride's door cannot open it while its queue's back is unjoined, see the recipe), run until the line of <thing> shows no parent but 0 (a wait that times out at 90 s prints `VOID`, saves and loads nothing and exits 3), `pause`, `savepark`, Load again. Each read is `scripts`' count and the thing's lines and the save and load gaps; each Load prints the `companions started` lines that Load itself logged (`none` if none; counted past that Load's mark); a frame follows the first Load, the close and the last (default camera `220 640 60 0`, the site of `buy <id> 20 60`); `<label>-saved1.TPWS` is kept for `lib/scriptrecords.py`. Run on a binary with the Q910-tip bug and one without, on the same file, it shows the stale child (the old build loads the closed ride's child again and counts `SAVE_PARK_SCRIPT_ENDED`) |
| `lib/effectstep.py <outdir> <bin> <park file> <effect> [ticks]` | Where an effect's emitters stand tick by tick after a load (Q928): the Full Simulation save copied in as the private player's newest, Load row 1 paused, then `step 1` and the console's `particles` rows of the effect each tick: `steps.txt` (slot, life, particles, the raw place, 64 to a unit) and `started.txt` (the log's `started effect` lines, the place over 1024). The control is a worktree of `main` built in its own folder; the original's side is the recipe "Where an effect is started, in both games" |
| `lib/stockscene.py <outdir> <bin> <dest.TPWS> <TICK> [command ...]` | A scene file made from the stock Lost Kingdom park, off-screen: entered, paused on the first tick at or past TICK, each console command given in turn (`sell 13`, `buy 1101 41 22 0`), the rides, shops and sideshows listed and the park saved to `<dest.TPWS>`. A stamp or a flag is then written into a copy with `parkfile.thing_set` |
| `lib/pathscene.py <outdir> <bin> <dest.TPWS> <TICK> <N> [X0 Y0 W [H]] [-- command ...]` | A scene file of the stock Lost Kingdom park with more developed cells, off-screen: paused on the first tick at or past TICK, `path X Y` for the first N cells of the W x H block at (X0,Y0) (default 19 x 20 at (60,15), bare grass; its column 79 holds track cells), each with a 0.15 s wait, then each command after `--` (`fire 28`, the guard), saved: 126 + N developed (`parkfile.py -d`). Count the file, not the replies; never run it beside `mutate.py` (late replies cross) |
| `lib/cellprobe.py` | exec'd after `parkrun.py`: the window point over a named map cell, found by moving the real pointer and reading `strip`'s drawn census while a tool shows one square under it (`find( (x, y), start )`, `probe()`, `drawn()`, `squares()`), so a real click lands on a cell the original's run used (Q433); with the path or queue tool ANCHORED, `strip_end_at( (x, y), start )` finds the point whose strip ends on a cell in line with the anchor, by stepping along the axis, and works under the locator's moving camera (Q540); its step signs assume the unrotated camera, and it cannot find an end on the anchor's other axis (it stops when the strip ends on neither of the target's lines) |
| `lib/cropgrid.py IMG CX CY HALFW HALFH OUT [--scale 8] [--step 10] [--mark Y,...] [--markx X,...]` | A frame's few pixels made countable: the box cut, enlarged without smoothing, a red rule every STEP frame pixels labelled with the frame's own coordinates, and a cyan line at each predicted row and column, to read a sprite's drawn top, bottom and edges against the marks in either game's frame (Q470, `q470/confirm.py`) |
| `lib/pickrays.py OUT BIN ORIGDIR [TAG]` | The original's own pick rays handed to this build's `pickray` (Q824): for each pixel of `ORIGDIR/<TAG>-scan<i>.log` (`hoverscan.py PID 99 any pick exact grid ...` at camera i, `met 0` pixels only) the eye `ORIGDIR/<TAG>-run.out` records for that camera (`== camera i ... eye X H Z`, read from `[0x00790aa0]`.. in full) through the original's P, and the answer's `exact` point and cell against P and `[0x007b05cc]`; one line a pixel, then the counts within 0.01 and in the same cell. Where the two pickers hold one land model the answer is P (the line through P meets the land at P), so a land model that differs shows as a DIFF, however the two cameras stand (`original/pickscan.sh PARK OUTDIR TAG "X,Z ROT;..."` writes the inputs, one frame and 14 x 10 pixels a camera; `SHOTS="2:640,240"` keeps this game's frame at an original pixel of camera 2, through `bothcam.py`'s mapping, which lands a cell off the original's own pixel: the claim is the rays'. A script of yours must not name anything `d`, `D` or `move`: `gamedrive.py` holds them) |
| `lib/bothcam.py X,Z ZOOM ROT [PID]` | One camera in both games, by the original's numbers (`camera.py`'s point, zoom and rotation in degrees): `console()` is this game's `camera X Z ZOOM -ROT` (its yaw is the original's rotation negated), `ours( reply, ... )` sends it from a confirm script after `parkrun.py`, `original( pid, ... )` runs `setcam.py` with the follow stopped, `both()` does the two; the command line prints the console line and, given a PID, sets the original. At the same numbers this game's 1280 x 720 pixel is (640, 360) + (the original's - (512, 384)) x 720 / 768 (Q440, Q441); `to_original( x, y )` is the way back (Q478) |
| `lib/parkfile.py` | The one reader of a park file: `channels( p )` (the ride system module's records' channel counts by item, the walker's reading, and each item's first record of more than one: a coaster's pylon outside the catalogue, five channels, which `ParkThingStates` took as one until Q810 (Q814); `parkfile.py -H <file>...` prints them; Q778, for Q779), `modules( p )` (the body's modules in the file's order with each one's start and end: the action recording, the seventeen tagged modules and the interface block; `parkfile.py -M <file>...` prints each one's length and the start of its md5, file beside file; Q249: which modules two park files share), `voices( p )` (the sound manager's voice list after the sound block: the category slots, the pool's blocks and each voice's handle, class, place, range, category, effect, variation and sample places, controllers and child; `effect_id( cat, index )` names the effect from its SFX map; Q560), `script_objects( p )` (every script's object list: type, handle, node, lookup, tag; Q560), `-N <file>...` prints the voices, each with the script record, the crowd's word or the rain's handle that keeps it; `coasters( p )` (the coasters module: the four counts, the first coaster's header, its partners and every piece record, cell on the 96-wide grid, loft, bank, incline, yaw, loop; `parkfile.py -K <file>...` prints it; Q593), `analyser_grid( p )` (the analyser's `+16` to `+62598`: the five grid dwords, the 1,024 blocks with their nine value and worked-out pairs, the four small rings, the six kept answers) and `sound( p )` (the sound block's three dwords, its length and whether it ends at `NUOS`; Q546; `q546/sweep.py` sweeps both over a list of files), `cellflags( p, x, y )` (a cell's mType and mFlags: `0x20` NOMODIFY, `0x40` outside the park; `parkfile.py -n <file>...` prints every NOMODIFY cell with its neighbours' type and flags; Q538), `cells( p )`, `hoarding( p, x, y )` and `show_map( p, X0, Y0, X1, Y1 )` (every cell's map type and track record, whether a cell is a hoarding, a character map of the cells by type, the hoardings marked; `parkfile.py -c <file> [X0 Y0 X1 Y1]`; Q469), `object_days( p, thing )` (an object's customers ring, State of repair and remaining life, and the all-items row's customers and bars of them, Q367; and its shops' and sideshows' columns: the takings less the costs rings' last thirty, the totals' difference, the satisfaction and its bar, Q368; `-o` prints every object's), `advisor()` (the advisor thing's message records not fresh; Q313), `advisor_forget( src, dst, message )` (a copy of a jungle file whose advisor has never said a message, its block packed again; Q316), `advisor_slots()` and `advisor_clocks()` (his eight slots, the messages waiting to be said, and his last action, kept message and message playing; `-a` prints all three; Q314), `clock()` (the clock module's two dwords, the clock and the second stopwatch; `-a` says how much of the advisor's wait the file was saved with; Q315), `Park` (things, cells, sprites), `track()` (the track-rides module, cars and riders), `models()` (every model record: item, flags, node words, lookup records, channels), `head()` (a sprite), `effects( p, i )` (a cell's five region-effect words) and `tickets( p )` (the golden tickets' cell measures: Global 3's covered cells and the Secret's watched per cent; `-T` prints them; Q591), `emitters()`, `emitter_words()` and `emitter_aim()` (the particles module's live emitters by their chains, where one is not its template, and its own velocity at `+0x38`; `parkfile.py -e <file>` prints them), `particle_density()` (the module's density, the live header's `+0x20`, which a park read from the file runs at; Q703), `tags()` (the interface block that ends the body: each aerial tag's id, text, life, picture, buttons, thing and item, with the bytes the original leaves unwritten; `parkfile.py -t <file>...` prints them), `thing_set( src, dst, thing, offset, fmt, value )` (a copy of a file with one field of one thing's record written over and the block packed again, answering the old value: how a state no screen of either game reaches is put in a file; Q325), `economy( p )` and `economy_set( src, dst, fields, loans )` (the economy thing, thing 8: the fee, balance, batch, withdrawals, last balance, red stamp and profit and the eight loans' eight fields each, read, or written over by name and packed again: a bought loan no screen of this game buys; `parkfile.py -E <file>...` prints them; Q524), `calendar( p )` (the park clock's five fields, the 28 bytes before the arrival block, with the date of the file's tick and whether the month and day are that date's, as every file the original saves past tick 0 holds; `parkfile.py -C <file>...` prints them; `q526/clocksweep.py <list>` runs it over a list of files and counts the original's apart; Q526), `weather( p )` (the weather thing, thing 7, every field by the FILE's offsets: the quality, the change's and the forecast's stamps, the forecast, the drops, the lightning, the thunder's count, the bolt's location or None for the debug fill, the voice's handle, the wind; `_tick_of( ft )` a stamp as a tick of the file's clock; `parkfile.py -W <file>...` prints them; `weather_set( src, dst, fields )` writes fields over by name and packs it again; `q528/wsweep.py <list>` counts the original's files' stamps, thunder and location; Q528), `analyser()` and `filetime( tick )` (the park analyser's last 76 bytes by the serialiser's names, its six calendar stamps among them, `mLastStaffHired` at the record's `+73524`, and the calendar on a tick as such a stamp; `parkfile.py -s <file>...` prints them beside the staff's newest `mTimeHired`; Q333), `sets()` (the message centre's 29 listener sets, who hears each message type, as a load puts them back; `parkfile.py -x <file>...` prints them; Q397), `vehicles()` (the arrival vehicles: the header's three slots and current vehicle, the object list, cell (0,0)'s chain and each slot's thing's record by field and raw; `parkfile.py -v <file>...` prints them; Q342), `lab()` (the research lab's record: the used projects, the five kinds' records, `mNumProjects`, `mWorkLoad`, `mMostExpensiveProject`, `mLabActive`; `parkfile.py -l <file>...` prints them, a `restart.INTS` as well; Q349), `staff_patrols()` (every member's patrol corners, `+476` and `+478`, with the whole map and no area named; `parkfile.py -p <file>...` prints them; Q380), `pool()` (the staff pool's 32 slots by field; `parkfile.py -P <file>...` prints those in use; Q422), `pool_set( src, dst, kind, valid=0 )` (a copy with `mValid` written on every slot of that kind: a pool with nobody of a kind to hire, which the advisor's row 17 asks for; Q422) and `ring_set( src, dst, [(thing, ring, days, today)] )` (a copy with objects' day rings written over, `OBJECT_RINGS` by name (`costs`, `takings`, `customers`, `walkaways`, `served`, `satisfaction`), the days newest first; a shop's walk-away share or satisfaction for the advisor's rows 238 to 257; Q713), `money_rings()` (the analyser's eight month money rings, the bank's balance at each close (no month so far), all

| `perf/bench.sh <outdir> <Release bin dir> <secs> [cmds]`, `perf/speedscope_top.py`, `perf/stackq.py`, `perf/alloctypes/` | The performance profile of the stock jungle park (2026-10-26): the Release build with `OPENTPW_PROFILE=1` (`FrameProfiler`, off without it: a line every 120 frames with the average and worst `Update`, the frames a second, the collections, the heap and the bytes allocated), `console.log` from the load and `guests` before and after. `PRESENT=immediate` (Mesa's `MESA_VK_WSI_PRESENT_MODE`) lifts the display's cap, since the vsync cap hides the cost (143.9 fps against about 800). `TRACE=1` takes a `dotnet-trace` of the middle of the run (`TPROFILE=dotnet-sampled-thread-time`, the default, or `gc-verbose`; `TDUR` seconds), the tools at `~/.cache/dn-tmp/tools/dotnet-trace-dir`. `speedscope_top.py` reads the trace's inclusive and self time by thread; `stackq.py <trace> <pid> <frame>...` the self-time leaders and a frame's callers; `alloctypes` (`dotnet alloctypes.dll <nettrace>`) the allocation ticks by type and innermost OpenTPW frame. Build `-c Release`: a Debug build's numbers are not the game's |
| `perf/samepic.py <outdir> <steps> <label>=<bin dir>...`, `perf/ab.sh <outdir> <before bin> <after bin> [secs]` | Is the picture of the stock jungle park the same in two builds, and what did the change cost (PERF PASS 1, 2026-10-27). `samepic.py` launches each leg on the desktop (`SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy`, the real game folder), sends `pause` as its first line and `park jungle` after 12 s, `step <steps>` (fixed 1/60 s frames), grabs the window twice (the leg's own control: a paused frame repeats to the bit), then reads `sweeps`, `guests`, `circle` and `drawn`; it prints the first leg against each other for pixels differing and census lines differing. Give the first two legs ONE build: their difference is the noise floor, taken out of the later comparisons (a mask of the differing pixels grown by 6, not their bounding box, which hid the very strip where overlapping people stand). **Two traps, found the hard way**: `GameClock.Ticks` is never reset and the lobby ticks 4 or 5 times before the first line is read, and the 248 ms beat guests are drawn between is `(Ticks % 8 + partial) / 8`, so a leg whose `ticks=` at the park's start differs from the first leg's is relaunched until it matches (the script does); and at `step 120` two guests (things 25 and 26) turn at random between launches of one build, so use `step 30` for a bit-exact comparison (0 pixels, 48 census lines equal) and `step 120` only masked. To prove a check is not hollow build the bug into a copy of the tree (`content/fonts` and `content/shaders` have to be copied beside it or the game dies on its first shader) and add it as a leg: the furthest-first order reversed leaves 80 pixels at `step 30`. Run with `review-codex/venv/bin/python -W ignore -u`. `ab.sh` runs `bench.sh` before, after, before, after (immediate present, so the display's cap does not hide the cost; `PAIRS=n` pairs, default 2) and then the after build's `gc-verbose` allocation trace (`ALLOC=0` leaves it out), into `<outdir>/before-N`, `after-N`, `alloc-after`. **A run of one build spreads by about 3%** (the same Release build read 1.20 to 1.29 ms in seven runs), so a change under 3% needs five or more runs a side; read each run's steady state from four seconds after the `[load]` line, since the lobby's frames before it are 0.4 ms |

Run it on a launch's `console.log` (clean: `SafePaths` cuts paths there) and on the same run's terminal `run.log` as the control, which is not cut and must hit. A leak put back (the scrubber not handed to `ConsoleLog.Open`, or the roster's names not handed to it) makes `console.log` hit too; for the names, enter a park through `parkrun.py` (`q751/names.py`) and give `--also ref bare`, the private folder's players |

Run with `review-codex/venv/bin/python -u`. The box's text is wrapped to 64 columns, so no line of it passes 450 px with the Xvfb's font, 7 px a column (Q912; SDL asks for any 12-point font, so another server's differs); the log's `Start failure: no message box, ... is set:` line (the box kept off) holds the message whole. Windows' box is the Sentinel's to photograph on that PC |
| `lib/lobbyoptions.py WORKDIR CASE FOLDER [gpu=LINE] [vc=N] [dispN] [clickN] [backN] [locksave] [lock=FILE] [odd] [size=WxH] [tick] [box=tick\|cross] [exit=cross] [ENV=VAL ...]` | One start of the build on a private game folder kept between cases (`WORKDIR/game-FOLDER`), the lobby's Options screen opened by real clicks (Escape, the menu's `b`, `control 1d4d3` the Video Card arrow, `control ffffffff` the tick), screenshots `lobby`, `options`, `click1`..`clickN` and the log's `Graphics card:`, `GPU:` and `Options:` lines (Q983). `gpu=` sets opentpw.cfg's gpu line, `vc=` Config.tcf's video card field, `ENV=VAL` the environment (`VK_LOADER_DRIVERS_SELECT=*lvp_icd*` leaves one device). Run with `review-codex/venv/bin/python -u` on the desktop. The Config.tcf is version, rendering, resolution, quality (byte 12), video card (byte 16). `box=tick` or `box=cross` answers RESTART GAME after a card change and screenshots `restart` and `after` (Q985; a tick from the lobby ends the game, the script waits for it and prints the exit code). `dispN` clicks the Display row's arrow (`control 1d4d2`) N times first, twice reaching Borderless from Windowed, and prints opentpw.cfg's `display` line and the window's size before and after; `exit=cross` follows `box=cross` with the Options screen's own cross (`control fffffffe`) and `screen-closed` (Q986); `locksave` makes the private `save/` read-only once the Options screen is open, so the start found it writable and the tick's writes fail (a read-only *file* does not: the save replaces it), and the tick is photographed as `after-tick` (Q986). `odd` appends two bytes to Config.tcf before the start, so the game does not read it whole and the tick keeps a copy of it first (OPTIONS FILE BACKED UP, Q998); with `lock=opentpw.cfg` the RESTART GAME box then carries the two titles OPTIONS FILE BACKED UP and SOME OPTIONS NOT SAVED too, the longest box the tick can make (Q935, Q938; use a folder name no earlier case used, the two bytes are added at every start). `size=WxH` sends the console's `size` once the lobby is up, so the window is that size before the Options screen opens (640x480, 1024x768; the least it goes is 512x384, `Window.MinimumSize`), and prints the size it then has. With `OPENTPW_TEXT_CENSUS=1` as an `ENV=VAL` the log holds each text's size in its box with `OVER` where it spills (`UiText`): the way to measure a box's fit instead of judging a picture (Q935). `lock=FILE` (`opentpw.cfg` or `Config.tcf`) makes `FILE.tmp` a directory once the Options screen is open, so that one file's write fails and the other's goes through (Q999; the RESTART GAME box with OPTIONS NOT SAVED in it, or none), removed at the end. `backN` (with `dispN` and `box=cross`) clicks the Display arrow N more times after the box's cross, so the second tick sets the display back, photographed `back1`.. (Q987). `OFFSCREEN_DISPLAY=:79` in the environment runs it on a private Xvfb drawn by lavapipe (Q987; lane B), the Xvfb ended with the run; it lists one device, and `VK_DRIVER_FILES=a.json:b.json` (two copies of `lvp_icd.json`) lists llvmpipe twice, so the Video Card row offers Secondary and the RESTART GAME box can be reached |

The original at Q479's camera A: its own tile on 84 of 122 cells (Q488). `ONLY=FILE` takes only the cells a file names; `PAIR=FRAME0` instead correlates each cell's luminance with the same cell in another frame, game against game (Q489: on path cells, where neighbours look alike and the tile match cannot tell them apart) |

`happygrad.wct` bar about 0.02 short** (its greyish-teal texels spread 30 or less): use `barsplit.py` for that one (Q369) |

Exit 0 only when the build exits 0 with no error, the tests exit 0 with none failed and the guard refuses nothing. `CHECK_DIR` keeps `build.txt`, `test.txt` and `guard.txt`. Needs bash (Git Bash on Windows; run on Linux only). `CheckCommitTests` drives it with a stand-in `dotnet`. Measured 2026-10-29 on `f7a9bc86`: 23 s without the game (2,646 of 4,312 skipped), 4 min 46 s with it (2 skipped), `save/` unchanged; a failing test in a class that needs the game shows without it as one more skip |

The stock park: no player's slot is clicked (a player's own park is richer and its figures differ). Counts by line, so a path made twice shows twice. Reads files; touches no game (Q832) |
| console `mem` / `mem gc` / `mem compact` | The running game's own memory account, one reply each: the kernel's figures for the process, the managed heap (`gc` collects first, so the figure is what is reachable; `compact` also compacts the large object heap once, the cost of the holes a load leaves), what the game asked the device for by kind (textures with every mip, vertex, index and uniform buffers, the render targets, the count of distinct pipelines, with peaks), `mem vulkan` (Q834; `VulkanMemory`, by reflection into NeoVeldrid's private fields): the manager's **live** bytes (what `_totalAllocatedBytes` is: blocks handed out and not freed), the **chunks** taken from the driver (64 MB mapped, 256 MB unmapped, kept until the device ends) with their free bytes and largest free block, and the device's **staging pool** (textures and buffers an upload went through, kept for the device's life); `docs/DECISIONS.md`, "NeoVeldrid's memory", and `mem wads`, the WAD archives the process holds open: how many, the bytes of their files held whole (0.0 since Q828, which reads an entry from the file when asked; an archive read from a stream still holds its bytes), and the entries cached, by whether they were stored compressed (Q827), with the entries asked for, the asks, the reads of the file and their time, the cached entries asked for once (0 since Q830, which keeps nothing asked for once; it measured the build before) and the six asked for most (Q830: an entry is kept from its second ask; the asks, reads and kept entries restart at each level's end, Q831, so a figure is the level's since the one before it ended). A `stats` right after `mem gc` reads a long frame: the collection's own stall is in its 240-frame window (Q954). `mem retire` (Q853): the texture views let go of after their frame's fence signalled, and those still waiting (0 once the frames after a leave have run) |
| `lib/capped.sh [-m SIZE] CMD...` | Runs CMD in its own systemd user scope capped at 6 GB with no swap (exit 137 when killed at the cap), so a test that runs away dies alone and the machine never runs out of memory; `mutate.py` and `gate.sh` run every `dotnet test` through it. A full test run with the game peaks at 1.5 GB |
| `lib/suiteloop.sh OUTDIR [N]` | Runs the whole suite with the game up to N times (10 by default) through `capped.sh`, each run's whole output in `OUTDIR/run-NN.out` and a line a run in `OUTDIR/summary.txt` (`STOP at NN` at the first failing run, `DONE` last); it does not build. Q942's tool for an unnamed one-off failure: the failing run's file names the test. Linux with systemd only (`capped.sh` uses a systemd scope). Sixteen runs (2026-11-01, Q866), ten plain and six with some generators seeded by a scratch patch, all passed, 4685 / 0 / 2, five minutes a run; start it with `nohup setsid` and watch `summary.txt` |
| `dotnet/seedsweep/` (`apply.py [--revert]`, then a build, then `run.sh profile OUT` or `run.sh sweep LO HI OUT [ONLYFILE] [CLASSES]`; `SWEEP_ALL=1` takes every test class) | Q1023's and Q1024's tool for a test that fails once in a thousand runs because a default `new Random()` was left unseeded: `apply.py` routes the product's 19 default generators and the 5 `Random.Shared` draws in 3 files through a scratch `SweepHook` (and copies in the scratch test `ZSweepScratch`), `profile` runs every test row once at seed 0 and writes `OUT.profile`, a line a row with its draws by generator (a row with `draws 0` draws from none of those, so a seed cannot vary it; `LobbyFlyer.Spawn` (`LobbyFlyer.cs:157`) seeds from `Environment.TickCount` and is not routed, but no test reaches it, only `Game` builds a lobby `Level`), and `sweep` runs the listed rows once per seed in one process (Q1024: about 11 s a seed for 121 cheap rows, 8 s for 13 dearer ones, 16 s a run for one row; two processes at once do not slow each other much), `OUT` a line a failure with its seed and message as it happens, then `DONE lo..hi` when the sweep ends (a sweep stopped early has no `DONE`, and one stopped before any failure has no `OUT` at all; `OUT.progress` says how far it got). The runner awaits async tests, binds `DynamicData`, `params` and optional arguments, gives `TestContext`, resets `Time.Now` and `GameClock` before each row and leaves out `ParkWeatherFreshTests`. `apply.py --revert` runs `git checkout --` on the 15 product files it edited, discarding any uncommitted edit of yours in them: commit that first. VK_INSTANCE_LAYERS=VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation`: `eval "$(vkvalidate.sh)"` before a harness launch, then `grep VUID run.log`. Add `VK_LOADER_DEBUG=layer,error` once to see `Insert instance layer "VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation"`: a run that prints no VUID line proves nothing unless that line is there (Q957: a manifest naming the library without a folder loaded nothing and said nothing). The stock park's autosave in the private folder, each run resuming the one before, off-screen printed 8 errors of `VUID-vkDestroyImageView-imageView-01026`, four after entering the park and four after leaving it (Q853: the loading screen's textures; none since the fence-gated retire). The layer has no in-use check for a descriptor-set layout or a shader module, so a run silent about those proves nothing of them |

Runs on the private copy (`gd run_python`), whose names lag the real project until it is refreshed; `--print` prints the code for one `mcp__ghidra__run_python` call on the real project. Grep the files instead of one tool call per function per kind |

Prints MID-INSTRUCTION, NO-INSTRUCTION, NAME-MISMATCH, NAME-NOT-IN-COPY or OUTSIDE with file:line; `mkev.sh` puts it in every review's evidence. | `lib/headseries.py <outdir> <bin> [ITEM [NAME]] [--photo ROLE:FRAME,...] [--load FILE] [--save-role R]` | This game's riders' heads across a ride's clips (Q848): the stock park, `ITEM` (default Mumbo, 1109) bought at (41,22) with its queue, five guests aboard, paused and saved as `Q848.TPWS` (the original loads it with `loadfile.sh`), then `step 3` and `rides` 450 times into `series.json` (the first channel and each head's drawn place and rest place; `Q848_STEP`, `Q848_COUNT`; `Q848_CAMERA`, the `camera` line the park is viewed by, default `430 237 90 0` (Q1009: `430 240 90 0` puts ours' eye at (430, 70.597, 184.179), the eye of q917's pauses, 184.2); `Q848_DELAY`, seconds waited before the guests are admitted, 0 (Q1008). The Ape's riders' heads are `RAND` draws and six runs, at a delay of 0 and 3, held the same eight heads (Head1, 02, 06, 09 and 12 to 15, never 03, 04, 05, 07, 08, 10, 11 or 16) with Head12 first aboard at frame 98, 13, 52, 88, 88 and 23: which of the eight is aboard at a frame varies, the set did not); off-screen. With `--photo` it runs on the desktop and shoots the window at each `role:frame` instead; `--load FILE` starts from a park file instead of buying (the row is the file's place in the list: off-screen the private folder held an autosave, row 2; the desktop's held none on 2026-11-04, row 1; the assert stops a wrong row; Q916, Q917); `--save-role R` (Q917) saves a bought ride's park once a poll finds its first channel in role R at frames 5 to 30 (the pause lands some 23 frames later; the role is read again and the run stops if it has left R; not with `--load`) instead of as soon as it runs; a head's lookup node (the census's leading number, not the original's record) is the fifth item of each head. The `rides` line names the Crazy Ape `'Ape Ride'` (item 1101), so `headseries.py <out> <bin> 1101 "Ape Ride"`; ours held 11 heads (Head02 to Head15) and the original's pauses name 13 records; the two games draw a rider's head from generators of their own, so they rarely hold the same head in one loop, and the heads ours saved for a park's riders were not the ones the original held on loading it (Q917: seven records on a file whose four riders had records 1, 11, 12 and 16, two of them held) |
| `lib/headcompare.py LABEL series.json pauses.jsonl... [--turn] [--xz] [--map FILE] [--max-gap F]` | `headseries.py`'s series against `headpause.py`'s pauses at the same (role, clip) and frame (Q848; ours linear between its two nearest samples; record r is head r + 1): each head's height, each two heads' horizontal distance and the maxima and means; `--turn` adds the rigid turn that best takes ours onto the original's per pause and each head's residual after it. Before Q915 the original turned Mumbo's ring and this game did not, so x and z were compared only after the turn; since it the fitted turn is near nought (0.22 degrees at most, Q915), and `--turn` shows how near. A record is the head numbered r + 1 unless `--map FILE` names ours' head for each record (Q916: a JSON `{"record": "HeadNN"}`, the Crazy Ape's lookup table having gaps; `lib/headpair.py` pairs them by REST place). Ours is read from consecutive samples that bracket the frame in any loop of the clip, each head from the first loop whose two samples both hold it, and never across more than `--max-gap` (default 4.0) channel frames; it assumes the clip repeats frame for frame from loop to loop. `--xz` adds each head's x and z against the original's (the turn not removed). A head that left ours between its two samples is left out, not allowed to shift the others; a record with no head of ours paired is said as that, not as a missing sample |
| `lib/headpair.py PAUSES.jsonl series.json... > map.json` | The original's head records paired with this game's heads by REST place (Q916): ours' rest table (name -> place, from every series given) against the original's heads read at its loop's end (frame 112 and on); a pair needs under 0.25 over x, z and height together, a record nothing of ours rests at is said on stderr, a record paired with two heads in two pauses is dropped and said. It GUESSES from places (the model's own record-to-node table, read in Q1008, replaces it for the Ape: `lib/ape-records.json`) and assumes the heads rest from frame 112 (`--from F` for another ride). The JSON is `headcompare.py --map`'s; for a ride whose lookup table has gaps (the Crazy Ape's: records 1, 3, 4, 7, 11, 12, 16, 17 paired with Head02, Head03, Head07, Head05, Head10, Head14, Head13, Head11 in `q916/ape-map.json`) |
| `lib/headpicmatch.py PAUSES.jsonl RUN.log... [--map FILE] [--max-gap F]` | This game's census `picture N` (`HeadOnRide`, ours' camera) against the original's sprite picture (+0xb8) per head per `headpause.py` pause (Q1003's `picmatch.py`, stored in Q1008): a head pairs when ours holds it at the same role and clip entry within `--max-gap` (4.0) frames, the nearest sample's picture; several RUN.logs pool their samples; a pause between two samples of ours that pick different pictures is printed `EDGE` and left out of the count. `--map lib/ape-records.json` for the Crazy Ape (record r is Head(r+1) without it). The head census line also carries, since Q1008, ` eye (x,y,z)` (`Camera.Position`, the park's axes) and ` towards (x,y,z)` (the direction `HeadFrame` picked by, in the node's frame, model axes) after `picture N`: with them a pick is worked out again offline (`headframe.py`'s `picture()`) from this game's own numbers |
| `lib/headframe.py LABEL pauses.jsonl run.log [--map FILE] [--samples N]` | Which of the 56 head pictures a ride head shows, by the original's stored matrix and by this game's (Q940; the census lines `matrix [...]` and `drawnturn [...]` are in `main` since Q947): `FUN_0044b510` re-worked offline (the view place taken into the node's frame, 7 rows by 8 columns of directions) on `headpause.py`'s `pictures` and `view` (record +4 matrix, sprite +0xb8, `*[0x790a98]`), checked against the original's own picture, then the same view through this game's REST matrix (`rides` census `matrix [...]`) and, where the census has it, its drawn turn (`drawnturn [...]`, nearest frame of the same role AND clip entry since Q1009); prints the picture each picks, the angle between the rotations and the share of 2000 spread camera directions that pick differently. Mumbo: record r is Head(r+1) (the census spells the first `Head1` and the rest `Head02`..`Head05`; the script looks up either); the Crazy Ape needs `--map` |
| `lib/headresidual.py PAUSES.jsonl RUN.log... [--map FILE] [--max-gap F] [--interp]` | How far this game's drawn turn (`rides` census `drawnturn [...]`) of each ride head is from the original's stored matrix, per `headpause.py` pause (Q947, Q1009): the angle of the rotation taking one onto the other and its axis in the node's frame (y near nought is a tilt of the node's up). Ours is the nearest frame of the same role AND clip entry within `--max-gap` (4.0); several logs pool; `--map` is `ape-records.json`'s or `headpair.py`'s record to head (the Ape's table has gaps; Mumbo's record r is Head(r+1)); `--interp` slerps ours between its two samples either side of the pause (a head's arm turns up to some 8 degrees a frame, so the nearest sample's residual is mostly the frame gap: the Ape's role 5 pairs read 0.0 to 4.8 degrees by the nearest sample, 0.0 to 0.84 interpolated); ends with the pair count, mean and max; needs no display |
| `lib/faceturn.py <data dir> [-v] [--order zyx\|xyz] [--edge point\|corner1\|offset]` | `FUN_0044a640`'s face turn at rest against each face-anchored node's own turn, over every `.md2` (Q1003): the kept turn at the anchor less its +2 word, the face's frame (n x e, n, e; e corner 0 toward the lerped point), Rz . Ry . Rx . F on the 4,096 table, in the parent mesh's space; the counts within 0.1 and 1 degree and the worst, and the layout check (the word zero exactly without `0x40000`, the turns one 16-byte table before the anchors). `--order xyz` and `--edge` are the controls; needs no display, about 10 s |
| `original/emitters.py PID SECS` | The original's memory: the particle system's live emitters by the used chain (slot, count, handle, effect, place, aim, life, particles alive, the four rates and the most as the density left them), a block a change headed by the live density (`[0x0080cef0]`, the loaded park file's), and the particle counts a second. A `POLL` for `loadfile.sh`. The place it prints is the start's units over 1024 (the emitter keeps them over 16, read over 64): an on-screen effect started at the gadget's tag, (5810, 0, 45947), reads (5, 0, 44) (Q638); `RAW=1` prints the place as the emitter holds it, 64 to a unit, where a start's fraction shows (Q928) |
| `original/scriptvar.py PID THING [INDEX [VALUE]]` | A ride script's variables in the running original, by thing: read, or **written** (the one tool that writes its memory). It is how a ride is broken on demand |

Compare with this game's `scripts`. Q849 |
| `original/object.py PID SECS THING [VAR ...]` | The original's memory: one object's `mState`, `mCanLoad`, the guest being loaded, the queue's head, the member assigned, the State of repair and the remaining life, and those variables of its script, a line a change with `mGameTick`. A `POLL` for `loadfile.sh` |

A `POLL` for `loadfile.sh`; it WRITES the original's memory (Q622, `q622/orig/run.sh`) |
| `original/loadtime.sh load <file.TPWS> <outdir> [n]`, `original/loadtime.sh restart <outdir> [n]` | A Load Park or a Restart Park timed in the original, off-screen (Q921): `load` starts the original through `loadfile.sh` (the file is the list's first row, entered once), then films the display (`ffmpeg`, `-use_wallclock_as_timestamps 1 -fps_mode passthrough -copyts`, 30 fps, 512 wide), logs `mGameTick` with wall stamps (`tickwall.py`) and does n timed Loads by Escape, the menu's row (510,50) and the list's first row (400,123), the press and release (held 0.1 s) in `press.txt` as `k press release`; `restart` does the same on the running original through the Restart Park row (510,228) and the question's tick (708,283). Which terrain meshes stand on half-lit land (0.225 long) and what light a mesh was given (Q619). `--axes` prints each mesh's own axis lengths instead (rows 0-2 of the node's `+0x10` matrix: what a scale key set), and `--poll S` reads them again every 30 ms for S seconds, each pass stamped with its time and the wall clock and the `=MODEL` instances looked up again, so it may start before the placing click (Q598) |
| `original/basevertices.py PID [MODEL...] [--verbose] [--as-original]` | How every placed instance's vertices stand on the ground in the original, against the conform and the drape (`park-engine.md`, "Where a placed object's model stands"): a line an instance with its made flags (`+0xe4`), root, its listed base vertices on their terrain corners or not, its draped meshes' spread (rise from rest less the ground under the rest place, one value when draped) and its interpolating lists' (`+0x98`) largest difference; then the park's totals. Read-only. Alexah's Full Simulation New Save: 917 on, 0 off, drape spread 0.0001, interp 36 listed (the station's 16, the Lava Jump's 20), largest difference 0.0000 (Q812; ours reads the same two numbers in `rim`, Q815). Run after the load has finished (`fsload.sh`'s `POLL`, 80 s in): read earlier, it reads the park the player started in. By default it passes over a node flagged `0x80000000`; `--as-original` walks as `FUN_00469a80` does (only `0x200` passed over): 925 on, 0 off in the same park, the other 8 the two coaster pylons' floors a saved node word marks `0x80000000` (Q816); it also counts more draped meshes (402 for 368) and slot 173's monkey one more other corner, the meshes flagged `0x80000000` without `0x200` |
| `original/facenormals.py PID [MODEL...] [--verbose]` | The original's memory, read-only: each placed instance's normals tables against its master's and against the face normals worked out again from the instance's own vertices: whether each table is the instance's own, the first run (the lighter's) unlike the master's, each face agreeing to 1e-4, faces naming a first-run entry, meshes marked `0x10000` (stale) and meshes flagged `0x1000000` (a colour stream at `+0x64`: the pulse or the hover's highlight, counted apart); the highlighted model `[0x007a1ce0]` and the pulse list `[0x007afe14]`. A line an instance, then the totals. Keep the original running past the read (`fsload.sh`'s `GRABS`): at 110 s Q819's `o2` lost its read halfway when the game stopped; its `run.sh` (`q819/orig`, a scratch script, not stored) defaults to 110 s and was set to 200 by `SECS=200`; its final form, which arms the readers on the second `POLL` call, was never run (the reads ran with an `fsload.sh` altered to do that), so use `fsload.sh`'s `GRABS` and arming as its row says. Alexah's Full Simulation New Save: 1,192 meshes (24 of them `cart.MD2`'s, which would not read and were never compared), all own, no face on the first run, the 2,542 faces that disagree all in the 60 stale meshes (Q819) |
| `original/modeltypes.py PID [WAIT]` | The original's model types (`0x007ae6a8`: id `+0xf8`, channel count `+0xe`, file name) and made models (the slot list `0x007a4614`: type, count, made flags `+0xe4`) after WAIT seconds, against `lib/modelchannels.py`'s rule: the types and models that differ. Read-only. Alexah's New Save by `fsload.sh`'s `POLL` at 80 s: 127 types and 462 models, 0 differ (Q814) |
| `original/pointsat.py PID HEX...` | Every dword of the original's writable memory pointing at the given addresses (`OFFS=N`: or up to N bytes before them), ~10 s: run it on its own output to walk a pointer chain up |
| `original/footprints.py PID [SECS]` / `PID items` | The original's memory: every placed model instance's footprint table as `ProceduralBase_SkirtFlags` built it (`[instance + 8] + 0x18`, `{ flags, frame }` a cell, the item's `.hmp` giving width and depth), byte 0's `0x40`, angle, covered rectangle and the heightfield words of its cells (frame << 16 \| word); `items` prints every loaded item model's table instead (the list at `0x007ae6a8`), placed or not, so one park reads them all. Run after `loadfile.sh load` (Q649, `q649/orig/run.sh`, `run-items.sh`) |
| `original/brk.sh ADDR[,ADDR...] [HITS] [SECS] [WORDS]` | Who CALLS a routine of the original: a hardware breakpoint through `gdb` (up to four addresses joined by commas, each hit naming its own; Q364), attached and detached again; at each hit `mGameTick`, the registers and the stack's words that point into the game's code, nearest first (the first is the caller's return address; read each in Ghidra, a real one follows a `CALL`). `watch.sh` says who writes an address, this says why. `BRK_PRINT='printf "fctrl %x st0 %.17g st1 %.17g\n", $fctrl, (double)$st0, (double)$st1'` adds one gdb line a hit: the x87's control word and stack at a compare. `BRK_COND='*(int*)($esp+12) == 0xd0'` makes every address a conditional breakpoint, so a routine called many times a second is caught only for the call wanted: at a routine's first byte `$esp+4` is its first argument, so this is `Sound_PlayEffect`'s effect, and with `0x0051bfc0,0x0051c130` it names who plays effect 208 (Q567, `q567/orig/run.sh`). `BRK_INIT='set $rf = 0'` runs gdb lines once before the breakpoints are set: a condition that reads a convenience variable nothing has set yet is an ERROR, and an erroring condition STOPS, so every hit counted (Q1005) |
| `original/flagangle.py PID SECS STEP` | The original's memory, read-only: the angle the gates' flags stand at, read every STEP s for SECS from the first flags face of the face list (`dynfaces.py`'s read): its corner [0][0], 0.5 off the pole (411.25, 22, 38.75), `atan2( dz, dx )` in degrees, a stamped line a read. With `thingpoke.py %PID% S 15 7:0x5c:f=W` it shows a change of wind land in one frame (Q643, `q643/orig/run.sh`) |
| `original/thingpoke.py PID SECS KIND THING:OFFSET:FMT=VALUE ...` | The original's memory, **written**: holds words of things at given values, written again whenever they read otherwise, a line a write; refuses a thing whose type byte (`+2`) is not KIND. `150 15 7:0x38:B=1` holds the weather's `mLightning`, so bolts and thunder come on a clear day. `thingwords.py` reads the same words. Stop the original afterwards |
| `original/boltpoll.py PID SECS [STEP]` | The original's lightning bolt records, read-only, polled every 2 ms: a line when a record's life left (ms), the live count or the packed grey changes, with the wall clock; `[0x008bcbac]`, count `+0x3308`, record `+0x3008 + 0x30 i` (life left at record `+0x24`), the frame's ms `+0x9d9c`, shape hash `+0xa198`, grey `+0xa19c`. A `POLL` for `loadfile.sh`; bolts on a clear day from `thingpoke.py PID SECS 15 7:0x38:B=1`. `q1044/orig/analyze.py` checks every word against the grey formula (`exe/weather.md`, "The bolt"). Q1044 |
| `original/boltaim.py PID SECS [DX DZ [STEP]]` | The original's memory, **written**: holds every live bolt record's ground point at the park camera's point of interest (the i-th record `DX * i`, `DZ * i` from it), its top keeping the lean the original rolled and its life, grey and hash untouched, so a bolt that would fall anywhere on the map falls in the frame. | `original/liftcells.py PID X,Y ...` | The original's two grids at the cells named, read once: each cell's MAP type (`[0x008023a0]`), its track type, the object `FUN_0053bf30` answers for it (a 0xc or 0x11 cell's by its parent) and whether that object's model holds a `.hmp` and where its root stands: what the track tool's square lift reads (Q775) and the path tool's (Q776); the path tool's squares over a kart piece in the original are Q776's recipe: buy the Dino Karts as `q775/orig/run.sh` does, Escape, click bare ground north of the mouth (it arms the path tool and anchors; the cell the click's pixel lands on varies by a cell, run O anchored (34,23), P3run (34,22): read the anchor back, `0x0081ede4`/`e8`, before the poses), then hover the mouth and read `squares.py`; a cell left of the camera's middle (28,22) needs `camto.py` moved first |
| `original/original.sh`, `tpwmem.py`, `gmove.py`, `record.sh`, `watch.sh` | Starting, reading, clicking, filming and watchpointing the original ("The original under Proton", below) |
| `original/timens.py SECONDS CMD...` | A command run in a time namespace where the computer has been up SECONDS: the original's park clock without a restart (warning 1, below). `original.sh start` runs the game in it, 60 s, by default (`ORIG_TIMENS=0` starts it plainly). Ubuntu's `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1` refuses it to a plain process (`PermissionError` on `/proc/self/setgroups`), so it runs under Steam's AppArmor profile, which allows user namespaces: `aa-exec -p steam -- python3 timens.py 60 CMD` |
| `original/kwinwl-start.sh` | A private display on `:78` drawn by the graphics card (`kwin_wayland --virtual` with Xwayland) for the original, where Xvfb draws by software. `:78` is `parkrun.py`'s too. **Not run since it was written**: try it before leaning on it |
| `lib/gamedrive.py` | What `parkrun.py` is built on: the launch, the console pipe, XTEST clicks and keys, the frame grab. Use it through `parkrun.py` |
| `fork-review-2/forkscan.py [--keep forkscan-<date>.json]` | Every fork in OpenTPW's network, three levels down (ours left out), through the logged-in `gh`: which forks are new and which branches moved since `forkscan-state.json`, each moved branch compared on GitHub with upstream's main and ours (a copy of a tip either holds is skipped; a fork of over 30 branches has only its default branch compared). Prints `WATCH` for a branch ahead of both, writes `forkscan-<date>.json`; `--keep forkscan-<date>.json` records that scan's tips once its moved branches are handed on, and scans nothing (a fork may have moved since). About 45 s |
| `fork-review-2/forkwatch.py [--fork OWNER/REPO[@BRANCH]] [--from SHA] [--keep watch-<sha>.json]` | The daily fork watch's first step (read-only): with no `--fork`, fetches the meneerkrabs fork's clone (`~/.cache/tpw-fork-krabs/repo`); with one, any fork `forkscan.py` names, cloned to `~/.cache/tpw-forks/OWNER__REPO`, its state `watch-state-<slug>.json`, its first look from the newest merge base with upstream's main or ours, its leads `watch-<slug>-<sha>.json`; takes the PC addresses in the lines added since the last look, maps each to its function by `funcs-all.tsv` (every function's ranges, dumped from Ghidra 2026-10-16), drops the functions our tree and the FileFormats clone cite now and those an earlier look handed on, and writes `watch-<sha>.json`; `--keep watch-<sha>.json` records that look's tip in the state once its leads go to a sweep, and fetches nothing |
| `fork-review-2/mkwatchev.py <watch-SHA.json> <dir>` | The watch's evidence builder: splits the leads into areas (advisor, ui-hud, audio-fx, world, other) by the fork file citing each most, and writes `<dir>/claims-<area>-N.txt` (the fork's citing lines, +-2, read from the watched tip, for the sweeps) and `<dir>/code-<area>-N.txt` (our Ghidra decompile of each function, for the verifiers) from `<dir>/decomp.json`, which a Ghidra dump script (`watch/2026-10-16/dump.py`, a model for the next) writes |
| `gen_addresses.py` | Regenerating `docs/exe/addresses.md` |
| `dotnet/cliptracks/` (a project: `dotnet run --project tools/harness/dotnet/cliptracks -c Release -- <data dir> [clip]`) | Every shipped clip's position tracks against its model (Q554): the counts (late first keys, last keys before the declared span, additive models, nodes under a moved node, a route in the same clip) and the lists `late.txt`, `past.txt`, `offrest.txt`, `nested.txt`, `adds.txt` in the working folder; with a clip's path, its declared span and each position track's keys and samples. Builds the repo's `OpenTPW.Files` in Release |
| `dotnet/rselisting/bin/Debug/net10.0/rselisting <file.RSE>` (`DOTNET_ROOT` set) | One ride script's listing: its variables by index, the variable it watches, and each instruction at its word with its operands, variables by name and branches as `->word`. The `.RSE` corpus (`CLAUDE.local.md`) is what to point it at; `lib/soundsweep.py` reads its output. A turn of a script with no park can be counted from it by hand (Q144: Coaster1's 993 gaps in 200 turns) |
| `dotnet/wadcat`, `strdump`, `nodenames/` | A wad's entries, a string table's lines, a UI wad's node names (the last two are the maintainer's, outside the repo). `strdump` prints every part of a row, a parameter as `{Pn}` (since Q372: a row that starts with one, as UITEXT 441 "{P11} hours", read blank before); name the table under the data folder, `Language/English/UITEXT.str` (it reads through the tree it is built on, which decodes each language folder through its own `MBToUni.dat` from Q710) |
| console `strings <table> <row>...` | A string table's rows in the running game, through the table's own folder's character table (Q710): `strings Language/american/THEMENAMES.str 0` answers `strings: Language/american/THEMENAMES.str row 0 of 4: "Lost Kingdom"`. The log's `Character table: <path>, <n> characters` says each character table opened, once each |
| console `scripts` | Every script the scheduler runs, one line each: `script ID thing T dir D name 'N' at PC parent P child C sound S`, a count first. A script with a parent is a `SPAWNCHILD` companion (it takes its parent's thing, `0x0055516e`); thing 0 is a `SPAWNSOUND` child's; read it beside `original/origscripts.py` and the park file's own list (`q241i/rsse.py` prints each record's handle, thing, links and counter). Q849 |

**A poller of the original's memory is the one thing still written an item at a time** (`q257r/orig/cars.py`,
`q257x/orig/smoke.py` for a car's smoke handle, its emitter and its node's place, `q257t/orig/chan.py` for a car's model's channel stamps, `q253/orig/look.py`; a model's heads are `original/heads.py`, above): each reads its own structures. Start from the newest that reads the same
structure, and hand it to `loadfile.sh` through `POLL`.

## Cameras in both games

Read this before aiming a click or comparing two pictures. Each fact names the item that found it.

| To do this | Use |
|---|---|
| Set this game's park view | console `camera X Y ZOOM YAW` |
| Read the original's view | `original/camera.py PID` (`--aim X,Y` gives a cell's pixel) |
| Read the original's lens | `original/lens.py PID` |
| Set the original's view | `original/setcam.py PID X,Z ZOOM ROT off` |
| Put both games on one camera | `lib/bothcam.py` (`both()`, or `X,Z ZOOM ROT PID`) |
| Stop or restart the original's follow | `original/setcam.py PID off` / `on` |
| Pan the original to a cell | `original/camto.py PID X,Y` (pointer on the screen edge; it stops a follow) |
| Watch the original's pan and its edge | `original/campoi.py PID 7 0.03` beside a held keypad key |
| Watch the original's camera reset | `original/campoi.py PID 4 0.03` beside Home or `KP_5` held 1 s after `setcam.py` (`q690/orig/run.sh`): the reset lands at the release |
| Watch what a key does behind the original's game menu or options screen | `original/keylatch.py PID 6 0.03` beside the key held before or after Escape (`q692/orig/run.sh`; Up, not a keypad key, whose release Wine loses) |
| Watch the original enter and leave first person | `original/campoi.py PID 22 0.03` across the pick, its click and C (`q626/orig/run.sh`) |
| Click a named cell in the original | `original/cellfind.py PID X,Y`, straight before the click |
| Click a named cell here | `lib/cellprobe.py` `find( (x, y), start )` |
| Find cells outside the park in the original | `original/outside.py PID` |

**This game** (`ParkOrbitCameraMode`):
- `camera x y zoom yaw` takes world units, a cell times ten, and the yaw in degrees; its reply is the state (`poi=... yaw= zoom= pitch= ground=`). Q257n
- Both games at one camera: copy the original's point, zoom and rotation from `camera.py` as `camera X Z ZOOM -DEGREES` (the original's third coordinate is this game's second, and its rotation is this game's yaw negated: at 90 the same eye is -90); `lib/bothcam.py` does it. Q435, Q440
- The eye is lifted by the ground's height under the point, so over high ground it is further than `zoom` from it. Read `placed`'s listener, do not work it out. Q264
- The camera aims at the ground: it cannot close in on water 30 units up. Q257s
- A person's drawn size in both games: the mechanics' locator (Ctrl+S, then the button `95f`, the original's at (360, 613)) puts Mike Cooper's place at the frame's middle at zoom 110 in both (w 77.782 there); read his picture by `circle 26` here and `spritepick.py PID where 26` there, and mark the frame with `lib/cropgrid.py`. Since Q500 his whole picture is drawn here with the locator open or folded, as there; `circle` ends with the depth he is tested at ("tested 106.19" at 110, his depth itself in first person). Q470 (`q470/confirm.py`, `q470/orig/run.sh`)
- **A person's own pixels and sides in both games** (Q475): here, under `pause`, a frame, then `sprites 0` (the people's pictures, balloons and bubbles not drawn, the pick's record kept), a frame, `sprites 1`: two paused frames are the same to the bit off the people, so every pixel differing by more than 2 is theirs; `circle THING` gives the origin's own pixel (`origin pixel`) and the picture's size, origin and reflection, and the sides are the origin's pixel minus and plus (origin x, width − origin x) × 60 / w × the frame's height / 768 (here w is `circle`'s depth × sin 45), swapped when reflected (`q475/confirm.py`, a `[W H]` for `size`). The original cannot hide its people: freeze the frame and its memory as one with `kill -STOP` on the game's pid, read `spritepick.py PID where THING` before and after the `import` grab, then `kill -CONT` (`q475/orig/run.sh`: before and after equal at 10 of 10), and read the sides by eye on a `cropgrid.py` crop with both rules' marks. **Never let the command that starts an off-screen run hold the words of `original.sh`'s display test** (`Xvfb` and the display number, in a note or a check in the same shell line): its `pgrep -f` finds that shell, the private display is never started, and KWin, `gmove.py` and `xdotool` then fail with "Can't open display: (null)". Q475
- **What a constant of the original's draw does, seen in one pose** (Q477): hold the clock with `memhold.py PID 14 0x007859a0:I=1` (the people stop, the frame keeps drawing; this sets only the clock's paused flag, not a real pause: while held the clock reads the capture of the last real pause, which can be earlier than now, so it may step back and then stand, and when the flag is put back the held span falls due at once, so take nothing timed just after it), grab a frame, hold the constant at another value with a second `memhold.py` for a few seconds and grab again, then once it is put back; the first and last agree off the corner the pause leaves moving, so the middle frame's difference is the constant's alone. Mike's depth pull `0x00702044` held at 0, -0.02, -0.05 and -0.2 under the mechanics' follow: `q477/orig/run.sh` (`KS="K K K"`, a cycle a value) and `q477/orig/trio.py` (the three crops with the origin's row marked, and the rows that differ). Our side's lowest drawn row of a person against the origin's row: `q477/ctl.py`. Q477
- **Who in the view is hidden by what is nearer** (Q500): under `pause`, `circle` with no thing lists every drawn person (window pixels, depth, picture, "tested" depth), and a frame with and one without people (`sprites 0`) give each one's own pixels; set against their picture's rectangle, the fewest are the ones something nearer covers. `q500/more.py` (`cut()`: the census, the eight most covered, a crop sheet of four; at the start view the park gate's stone hides two), with first person's census (`camcorder`) after. Q500
- `freeze` stops only the lobby's orbit. `pause` stops the clock, and nothing eased on `Time.Delta` moves under it: `step` before a grab (`VERIFYING.md` rule 91). `camera` then `step 2` moves every walker two ticks, so take a census last. Q257r
- `cellprobe.py` needs the camera set first (`camera X Y 60 0`): at zoom 25 a 50 to 90 px step stays inside one cell and the slope is nought. Anchor nothing before both corners are found. Q433

**The original** (1024 x 768 off-screen frame at its origin):
- `camera.py` reads the point `0x007908f0` (x, ground, z; a cell is 10), zoom `0x007909ec`, rotation `0x00790a38` and the drawn rotation `0x007909e0` (eased after it), the camera matrix `0x0087aff0` (its last row the eye) and the draw matrix `[0x0087b088]`. Q435
- Its eye is the formula's: point + (-h sin r, sin p x zoom, -h cos r), pitch from the zoom (`park-engine.md`, "The park camera"), measured at rotations 0, 90 and 180. Q435, Q440
- Its arrow keys turn the view and do not pan. The pointer past 0.95 of the way to a screen edge pans (only with `[0x0078d910]` clear, an Options toggle), about 8 cells a second, and stops a follow: `camto.py` learns the sense and rate, then rests the pointer in the middle. A pointer left there by a script keeps panning (Q440's first round lost two rotations to it). Q433, Q439, Q440
- `setcam.py` writes the camera words (with `off`, or a follow puts the point back the next frame) and reads the eye back: 0.000 units off the formula at every rotation tried. Q440
- **Its zoom stands between 70 and 130**: its update clamps it, so `setcam.py` given 150 reads back 130 while this game's `camera` takes 150, and the two pictures differ by 150 / 130 (pixels mapped from one land elsewhere in the other: Q478's first round met the Litter Bin for the Drinks Shop). Keep a shared camera's zoom inside 70..130. Q478
- A file entered by `screenpark.sh` is framed on the camera's own point; the Load Park list's view is 65 units on. `parkrun.original_view( x, y )` allows for the list. Q359
- For the first seconds after a load the camera is not on the saved view: poll a short-lived thing, do not film it. Q257w
- A camera file's listener is not where the camera was aimed: read `voices.py`'s first line. Q263

**The follow**:
- It is `gui_CameraFlags` `[0x00790ab0]` bit `0x1000` (`park-engine.md`, "The follow"). The original starts it when the locator's button finds somebody, on Ctrl+S or Ctrl+V with a person's window up (with nobody it opens a locator and leaves the bit clear), and on a window's binoculars, which put the window away; the point then trails the member as he walks, the arrow keys only turning the view. Q435, Q440
- A person's window, from the strip or the world, folds the locator and stops it: with a window open the camera stands still (Mike walked 12 units in 2 s, the point did not move). Q440
- It ends on any pan (the keypad, the pointer past 0.95 of the way to an edge: a second at the right edge left the point 83 units from Mike), the locator's fold, a mode change, and a person's window opening (the strip's click folds the locator, so the camera stands still while the window is up; a frame from before that click is not the view at a PICK UP). `setcam.py PID off` ends it from outside, `on` starts it again. Q432, Q435, Q440
- This game: `ParkLocator.Press` sets `Following`; each frame `Camera` puts the point on the member's blended place. A change to the camcorder's first person, a person gone, or a step that finds nobody clears it. A staff window's preview click does `GoToThing` once. Whether opening a window from the world click follows here is not checked.
- A walker's place against the camera is read under `pause`: two replies apart he has walked most of a cell. Q424

**The lens** (Q260):
- Every view hands the projection an angle (the park 90, the lobby `ISLANDFOV`), and the draw matrix's fixed screen scale makes the picture `2 atan( tan(a/2) / 2 )` high: the park 53.13 x 67.38, the camcorder the same, the lobby at 100 61.58 x 76.93 (`park-engine.md`, "The lens"). `original/lens.py PID [SECS]` reads it live; this game logs it once a mode (`Camera: ParkOrbitCameraMode lens 53.13 high at 4:3, ...`). Q260, Q441
- This game draws the same lens (Q441): its 1280 x 720 pixel is (640, 360) + (the original's - (512, 384)) x 720 / 768 at the same camera, six named cells on the first probe at three rotations (`q441/confirm.py`), and its middle 960 x 720 scaled to 1024 x 768 lays on the original's whole frame (`q441/sidebyside.py`, `q441/side-by-side.png`). The scripts of Q260 to Q440 (`q260/confirm.py`, `q435/confirm.py`, `q436/frames.py`, `q440/confirm.py`) were written for the old 90 degree lens and use x 360 / 768 and the middle 480 x 360: change both before running one again. Q260, Q435, Q441
- A change drawn at one camera is shown by the box difference against an earlier run's frame at the same numbers. Q436
- One first-person stand in both games (Q485): the original's way in (Q496's, below) stands its eye at (481.897, 5, 166.285) looking (0.2788, 0, -0.9604), and `camera.py PID` there prints the first-person draw matrix and eye (`q485/orig/b-camera.txt`); here `pause`, the pointer at the window's middle (Steer turns the view off it), then `camcorder 481.897 166.285 -2.85905` (the yaw is the original's rotation negated, as `bothcam`'s). A ground point put through that draw matrix lands on ours by the same 720 / 768 map: `q485/linefit.py`. Q485
- The original's records do not depend on its camera, so a run whose camera went elsewhere still gives them. Q436
- The original was once read as standing "further back" (Q257r, repeated by Q264) and once "closer" (Q357) at the same numbers. "Further back" was the Load Park list's view, 65 units on (Q359); "closer" was the lens: the eye is the same (Q435) and the picture showed the middle half each way against OpenTPW's old 90 degree lens, so everything stood twice as large in it (until Q441). Q260
- `camera.py --aim`'s pixel was cellfind.py's first probe at both of Q260's cells, with the camera still: the cells out at Q439 came from something other than the lens. Q260

**Aiming a click** (every one of these cost a void):
- Set the camera first (`setcam.py ... off`, or `bothcam.py`), then aim with `camera.py --aim` and click through `cellfind.py` (`PICK=1` with a window open): six of six on the first probe, and a click on a named cell with Mike's window open landed on it (the picker 4018 = (49,31) at the press). Q440
- Read the camera in the same run as the click, straight before it. A fixed pixel or cell from an earlier run named other cells (Q435 O1, Q438 O3).
- Aim relative to the camera's own point, not to fixed cells (`q438/orig/run.sh` `AIMREL=1`); better, take any cell of the right kind found live (`outside.py`, `cellfind.py`) and never hard-code Q433's (51,27), (54,26), (45,15).
- A cell off the frame stops the run unclicked. Q439 stopped at 877,38 and at 2003,686.
- A frame and its camera read seconds apart are not the same view while a follow runs. Q438
- Stop the original on any exit of the run script, or the next run loads into it. Q439
- The original's picker reads straight off the pointer: `camera.py --aim`'s pixel, one `xdotool mousemove` on `:77`, and 1.5 s later the cell `[0x007b05cc]` and its hit point `[0x007a1c70]` (three floats, x ground z), which stand unchanged where the ray meets nothing (`q472/orig/run.sh`). Q472
- The pixel map to this game holds only where both points stand on the same ground: at (400,780) this game's camera stood on ground 7.0 and the original's point slid to (400, 781.6) after `setcam.py`, and the mapped pixel fell two rows short. There, find this game's pixel by `pick X Y` down a column (`q472/confirm.py`, G6b). Q472

## Recipes

Each was found by trial in the item named, and cost a run to find.

- **The lobby's bolt in both games, and its flash frame** (Q1037, `q1037/orig/run.sh OUTDIR [SECS]`, `q1037/ours/run.py <out> <bin>`): the original on the desk as `lobbypanel.sh` (player `2fsfresh`, one slot click), then `lobbystrike.py` holds the nearest island's lightning mask at 63, `boltpoll.py` reads the records and ffmpeg films the picture (`-i :0.0+320,0`, 1920 x 1440 scaled to 1024 x 768, 30 fps, no pointer); 13 strikes in 26 s. A strike's flash is the frame whose whole-picture mean jumps +21..+28 for one frame (`orig/analyze.py`, grey means per frame; the bolt's first read in `bolt.log` against the film's `film.t0`, good to a frame). Ours on the desktop: `q1034/confirm2.py` without `OFFSCREEN` (`attract off`, `island 0`, `settle`, `freeze`, `pause`, `near` says `axisDist=-`, `strike`, `stepsweep`), the bolt's width as runs of pixels whose smallest channel is over 215 inside a box above the water. Ours' strike falls at x 795 of 1280 on island 0 (axisDist 72.0), the original's wherever its generator puts it.
