- **The lobby's bolt in a frame, at exact ages** (Q1034, `q1034/confirm2.py`, off-screen): `attract off`, `island 0`, `settle`, `freeze`, wait 2 s, `near` must say `axisDist=-` (island 0 never strikes by itself, its census `strikes/s=0.000`; a strike caught before `island 0` is the attract wander's, and a `step` on an island that does strike can strike by itself; a strike before yours moves `Lightning`'s own random sequence on and the bolt lands elsewhere, as does every bolt frame drawn before it, each taking a draw from the same generator: the script throws the run away and repeats), `pause`, then `stepsweep( "near", "near flyers", 20, "strike", "strike" )`: a frame and the census every 0.05 s of the bolt's own age. A bolt is drawn at age 0 already (a grey of 192..255 plus a draw, Q1044), so measure against a frame grabbed BEFORE `strike`. A threshold on one frame takes flyers into the mask: keep the pixels lit in the frames at ages 0, 0.05 and 0.1 s all three (`mask_of` on each, `&`), in a box above the water (the ocean moves 25,000 px a second). Island 0's bolt stands at about x 790..830 of the 1280 x 720 frame, partly behind the player panel; the box that holds it is (740, 0, 900, 340). Until Q1044 the opacity was `life^2 x (0.55 + 0.45 |sin(47 age)|) x F` (F the fade from the camera, 0.993 here); now the census's `opacity` is the brightest channel of the grey the bolt is drawn in over 255, times F (the original's saw-tooth of 192..255 plus a draw of `rand`, `exe/weather.md`, "The bolt"), with `slots=`, `slot0=<life left>ms` and `rgb=` beside it for the first live bolt, and the old curve is `flash` alone, which still moves the lobby's sun: a frame's grey is a draw, so match a frame to its own census line (hold the clock: the grey is kept while only the debug console's pause holds it, and a `step` draws a new one). A control with the lobby's 0.42 put back is a copy of the tree under `~/.cache/dn-tmp/` with `BoltLifetime = 0.42f`, built there (`git archive HEAD | tar -x -C <dir>`).
- **A test's flake found by setting its private generator from outside** (Q1022, `q1022/`): give the class a set-only `internal Random` seam (`ParkAudio.ScriptRandom`), then in a scratch test method (not committed) loop `for ( seed = lo; seed <= hi; ++seed )`, set the seam to `new Random( seed )` inside the helper that builds the object, call the suspect test method directly in a `try`, and `File.AppendAllText` the seed and the assertion's first line on each failure; one process runs about 14 seeds a second for a park-audio test (7,500 in 9 minutes), where `dotnet test` once per seed costs 3 s each. Log the state the assertion reads (here `ListEmpty`) for every seed, not only the failing ones, so the cause is shown as 17 failures of 17 with it and none of the rest. Fix by a seeded generator in the test's helper; the same seeds put back (2199, 2307) are the cause put back.
- **Finding which tests a default generator reaches** (Q1023, Q1024, `q1023/`, `q1024/`): before sweeping, profile: `seedsweep/apply.py`, build, `SWEEP_ALL=1 seedsweep/run.sh profile OUT` (all 5,109 rows in 4.5 minutes), then `grep -v 'draws 0 ' OUT.profile`; only those rows can differ by seed, so sweep only them (Q1024: 158 rows of 152 methods; `SWEEP_ALL=1 seedsweep/run.sh sweep LO HI OUT ONLYFILE`, one process a cost class so the dear rows do not hold the cheap ones back: the lists were `q1024/only-light.txt`, `only-mid.txt`, `only-mon.txt`). Traps found: the runner must reset every clock a row's test reads before each test (`Time.Now`: a float that grows past 2^18 s stops taking 1/60 s steps, and five tests fail on every seed after the eighth pass; `GameClock.Ticks`: it grew 2.0 million ms a seed and `ParkTickTests.EveryWalkIsNoticedWithinOneTickOfComingDue` failed by 32 ms from seed 194 on), either of which looks like a seed and is not; a runner that does not await an `async Task` test lets it run on and publish into the next row (`UpdateChecker`'s offer); a row that counts on fresh objects' unseeded rolls differing (`ParkWeatherFreshTests`) fails under the hook by construction; `Random.Shared` is not a default generator the hook can see unless `apply.py` routes it (it does since Q1024); `Shared` is one stream a seed across all rows, so a failure in a `Shared` row (`CameraLensTests`, `LobbyEyeHandoverTests`) is reproduced by re-running the same `ONLYFILE` from that seed, not by the row alone. Prove the runner can fail: the held-chain test of `0606a264^` (`git show 0606a264^:source/OpenTPW.Tests/ScriptSoundTests.cs`), `SWEEP_RAW=1`, an `ONLYFILE` of that one method, seeds 2199 and 2307 must fail with "not before the newest child's time" and pass at 942 (`q1023/control-*.txt`; not re-run on the Q1024 runner; Q1025 re-runs it first). A failure in a long sweep that starts at one seed and holds at every seed after is the runner's, not a seed's.
- **A script's speed word read in the running game** (Q875, `q155-b16/confirm.py`, off-screen, `exec`s `parkrun.py` and `parkscene.py`): `scripts` prints each script's `word`, `lastwait` (the milliseconds its latest `WAIT` armed for), `lastanim` (the milliseconds its latest `TRIGANIM`, `TRIGWAITANIM` or `TRIGANIM_CH` armed `WAIT4ANIM` for, Q1060: the jungle `junspray` sideshow, thing 14, reads 3699 at word 50 and 3362 after `scriptword 14 60`; `q1060/confirm.py <out> <label>`) `queued` (channel 0's queued speed, the scheduler's write after each due turn, Q1061: it follows `scriptword` within a tick or two of the script's next due visit, 1.100 for the Belly Bounce at entry; `q1061/confirm.py <out> <label>`) and `rate` (channel 0's play rate, 3 decimals, `-` with no model; Q1039: the Belly Bounce reads 1.100 at entry from the file, 1.300 at its first trigger after `scriptword 13 80`, 1.000 after `scriptword 13 50`; `q1039-b19/confirm.py <out> run0|run1`, the control and the final run), `rides` the word and the scream's `speed`, and `scriptword <thing> [value]` writes or reads the word alone (the ride window writes the thing's operating speed too). The Belly Bounce (thing 13): `word 60`, `lastwait 454` for its `WAIT 500`, 500 after `scriptword 13 50` once the next WAIT arms (wait 6 s); its `screaming, band B effect E speed S parameter P` starts read `speed 60 parameter 40`, `speed 80 parameter 50` after `scriptword 13 80`. A Hot Pot bought by `parkscene.hot_pot()` (thing 43): `bumpers` reads `performance 60`, and after `scriptword 43 80` and 3 s `performance 80 thrust 11 friction 1000 turn 12 restitution 1080`. A check that filters the `scripts` lines must match `]\s+script N thing`, as every line begins `[dbg]   script` (the first run's miss).
- **A mutant run whose `dotnet build` waits for ever** (Q868, `q868/mutants-leg.py`): the plain build joined a shared MSBuild or compile server that had been left half-dead (a zombie `dotnet` whose threads sat in `softleaf_entry_wait_on_locked`; `ps` and `pgrep` then hang reading `/proc`, so list processes with a loop over `/proc/*/comm` under `timeout 2`), the python waited on its pipe, and the mutated file stayed in the tree. Build every mutant with `MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE=1 DOTNET_CLI_USE_MSBUILD_SERVER=0 dotnet build -nodeReuse:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false` and give the build a timeout; `kill -INT` the python (its `finally` puts the file back), then `git status` before anything else.
- **Whether a put-back arithmetic bug can be killed by shipped data** (Q868, `q868/probe-final.out`): a scratch test stands every shipped walk model's nodes at random float origins and four turns (`RideNodes.Place( origin, angle )` takes any origin, not just a cell's), keeps the legs whose rest length is within 0.03 of a whole number, and prints where each mutant's truncated leg parts from the engine's. A form that parts nowhere in 22 million placed legs (184 near-whole legs, four turns, 30,000 origins each) is taken as an equivalent mutant (a sample, not a proof) (said so in the item, not tested); one that parts gives the test its input. The probe is not stored: it is 80 lines over `RideNodes`, `RideAnimations` and the `Leg` formula.
- **The ride window's own button, pressed in ours** (Q153, `q153/ours.py`): `openthing <thingId>` opens the window, `control <hex id>` prints the button's `middle (x,y)` in window pixels (0x3e34 the queue button, 0x3e2c pick up), and `click x y` is the real press, so the handler's own code runs (its log line `Ride window: ...` lands in the run's `console.log`); `tool` then says the mode and anchor. The console's `tool queue <thingId>` only arms the tool and anchors nothing, so it is not the button. `delqueue` refuses a placer's node (NOMODIFY), so a bought thing's entrance keeps its link in play; `cell X Y` reads the neighbours, direction byte, tile and `overlap` counter of the cells before and after.
- **A person's puff, in both games** (Q900, `q900/orig/run.sh`, `rund.sh`, `rune.sh`, `q900/confirm.py`): `puffpoll.py` beside the original from `loadfile.sh load` (stock park, off-screen), then the dismissal by Q432's clicks (`q432/orig/run.sh`: Ctrl+S, the mechanics' button, the strip, FIRE, the box's tick) and `matchgone.py` on its log; for the height, `holder.py` at (505, 585) from before the window opens (the window takes 7 s, hold 30), for leavers `miserable.py PID 90` and 260 s; the frame of a puff is `import` straight after the tick's `mouseup` (a puff lives 30 ticks, 0.9 s; `click()` sleeps 1.2 s, too long) with the camera on the member by `bothcam.py X,Z 70 0 PID` just before the click. In ours `confirm.py <out> <bin> fire|leave` (`OFF=1` off-screen): `fire` is the window path and a member carried to the plateau with `pickup`/`putstaff 50 58` and fired; `leave` is `happy 0` and the log line `gone from the stop` with `particles` read then (its lines end `raw (x,y,z)`, the emitter's words); the console log's `Person puff:` line names the person's place the puff was made for.
- **A person on raised ground, in ours, voicing** (Q847, `q847/scene.py`, `scene2.py`): the stock park has a flat plateau at ground 20.0, cells (28..70, 58) (the console's `ground X Y` reads it; `probe.py` there scans a grid), and no guest walks on it. `pickup 30`, `putstaff 40 58` (staff 30, 28, 27, 26, 25 are the stock park's five) put a member there under `pause`; `resume` and their idle draws sound within a second, each a "Park audio: staff effect" line whose `at (x,y,z)` is the sound's place. `scene2.py` saves (`save()`), finds the five staff sprites in the file by their `+0x88`/`+0x90`, writes `+0x8c` (a sprite record is 280 bytes from `pk.Park.sprite[slot]`), and loads it through the menu (`load()`): the way to give a person a sprite offset; neither the original nor ours keeps one on a walker (the original overwrites it with nought each frame, ours reads none for sound or the draw). The listener follows the camera, so gains from two runs are not comparable.
- **A guest on raised ground, in ours, voicing** (Q1020, `q1020/scene.py`, `scene_till.py`): under `pause`, `admit 40 58` makes a guest on the plateau at (405, 585) (`admit` answers the id; ids that divide by 16 yawn when the chooser finds nothing, by 8 are put off at their feet from a queue); `resume` a few seconds and the yawn is a "Park audio: yawn ... at (x,y,z)" line. For a put-off, poll `peeps` until a guest whose id divides by 8 is `InQueue`, `pin <id> 405 585`, then `sell <the ride>` in the same pause: a "Park audio: put off" line at the pin. A pin is no use for the till (the dismissal puts the guest at the thing's exit first, Q873): `move 16 42 58` sells and rebuilds the shop on the plateau (a new thing id: a `send` to 16 then fails, the admitted guests choose it themselves) and the first guest to pay gives a "Park audio: charge" line there. `scene.py <out> <bin>` and `scene_till.py <out> <bin>` do all three off-screen; the unit side is `ParkGuestSoundHeightTests` (the heightfield put under `ParkGround.Current` as an uninitialised entity).
- **A person standing where nobody walks, in the original** (Q839, `q839/orig/run2.sh`): the sprite's draw reads `+0x88`/`+0x90` each frame and stores what it drew in `+0xe4`/`+0xe8`/`+0xec`, so `spritehold.py PID points.json` writes one guest's place at each point until the draw has it and reads the height; `--stay X,Z,SECS` in the background (`&`, then `import -window root -display :77 -crop 1024x768+0+0 OUT.png` a few seconds in) holds the guest there for a frame, with `setcam.py PID X,Z 70 180 off` over it first. A `brk.sh 0x005420f3` with `BRK_PRINT` reading `$esi` and `$st0` reads the same sampler per hit but only for the places people walk, which in the stock park is flat ground (all 0). Stop the original afterwards: the writes are not undone by anything but the person's own tick.
- **A person's sound on raised ground, in the original** (Q856, `q856/orig/run.sh`, `HOLD=1`, `PHASE2=1`): a staff member PICKED UP and clicked down on the plateau stays carried (he follows the pointer, `+0x8c` 25.000), so hold a walking one there instead: `holder.py PID 505.0 585.0 SECS 6` writes the mechanic's place on the plateau (the draw's `+0xe8` then reads 20.000) with `bothcam.py 505,585 100 0 PID` over it, and `personsound.sh plateau 60 100` beside it prints each sound it makes there (a walking mechanic gives about one in 4 s; the game runs free, only a plateau hit stops it). `personsound.sh riders 40 150` for people the person object holds (the tills' guests in the stock park; no seated rider came in 106 s, run j, stopped by its 40-line cap). A `pkill -f` of the run's script kills your own shell: stop the original with `loadfile.sh stop`. Ours: `q856/ours.py <out> <bin>` is Q847's `scene.py` with staff 30 first at cell (50,58), off-screen.

- **A balloon and a person at a flagged cell, in both games** (Q948, `q948/`): the original is `fsload.sh` on a copy of Alexah's `New Save.TPWS` (the Full Simulation player: `OPENTPW_FULLSIM_PLAYER` set) with a `POLL` of `q948/poll.sh` (the sprite table by `spritetable.py`, then `spritehold.py PID points.json SLOT --setoff 0`) or `q948/poll-frames.sh` (`setcam.py PID 595,355 70 180 off`, a rest frame, then walker, balloon and both held by `--stay`, a frame each); a POLL that must wait for the load sleeps 50 s first. Ours is `q948/ours.py <out> <bin> <hold.txt>` on the Q817 recipe (`fs_profile`, a copy of the file as the newest, `enter()`): `ground X Z` at each held point, then under `pause` the console's **`pin <guest> X Z`** stands a guest (a balloon holder, from `guests`' `balloon held by N`) on the world point, and the camera `bothcam.console( 595, 355, 70, 180 )` frames it. Put the original's stock-park dumps beside the New Save's before saying a cell's flag is the level's: the New Save's live words differ at the cells a thing's or queue's footprint is stamped on (`park-engine.md`, "The footprint stamp clears `0x800`"); `q949/poll-cells.sh` (a `POLL`: the whole live map, then the camera over (330,290)) and `q949/d1.py STOCK LIVE` (the cells that lost `0x800` and the stamped ones, counted) are Q884's. **Ours' footprint flags (Q949, `q949-a3/`):** the console's `cellflags` prints the census (cells carrying `0x800`, flagged at load, cleared since, held by a stamp, the stamped counts of objects and queue pieces) and, as `cellflags rows`, every cell's live word (a stamped cell starred) a row a line; `q949-a3/ours.py <out> <bin>` loads the New Save as Q948's does, compares ours' `0x800` with `q949/orig/run1/cells.txt` at all 8,160 cells and its stamped set with the original's `0x200` cells, reads the 279 held points of `q948/orig/run3/hold.txt`, and pins a balloon holder at (339,299) under a camera on it (`sprites 0` isolates the people's pixels); `q949-a3/ours2.py <out> <bin>` buys and sells a Litter Bin and a Staff Room on flagged ground and compares the whole map before and after; `q949-a3/mutations.py` is the bug list for `mutate.py`. The original's flags in play are Q886's (below).
- **A path laid over a stamped queue cell, in both games** (Q841, `q841/`): the file is `q841/Q841.TPWS`, the New Save (`q948/newsave.TPWS`) with `parkfile.cell_set` making (31,17) and (31,18) loose ends (`mNeighbours` `0x44` to `0x04`: the path verdict lays over a queue cell only with exactly one neighbour bit, on a queue cell; the New Save's four flagged queue cells hold two each, so none is a loose end as it stands). The original: `q841/orig/start.sh <outdir>` (`q885/orig/start.sh` on that file, **left running**), `q841/lib.sh`'s `cells N`, then an aim (`setcam.py PID X,Y*10+5 110 0 off`, the pointer to (512,300) then (512,384)): aim on grass (31,15) and click (the path tool arms, mode 1, anchor 31,15; the run's cells before the first queue cell must be ground), aim on (31,18) and click (the run is laid and the tool put away); `savepark.sh` (`PLAYER=2fsfresh`) and `celltypes.py Q841.TPWS after.TPWS` read the types, `cells N` and `cellsdiff.py` the flags. A click with the hand empty on a queue cell hands it to the queue tool (mode 3), not the path tool, so the path tool is armed on grass. Ours: `q841/ours1.py <out> <bin>` (the Q888 recipe; `path X Y` for each of the four cells; it compares `cellflags rows` with the original's `cells-0.txt` and `cells-1.txt` at all 8,160 cells, the `0x800` bit and the stamp). `q841/mutations.py` is the bug list for `mutate.py`. **The lift** (Q846, `q846/orig/start.sh`, `lib.sh`, `ours2.py`): `start.sh <outdir>` is Q841's with its own file copy and `lib.sh` points `D` at `q846/orig/run1`; after the run is laid (the pointer on (31,15), click; on (31,18), click: pixels (512,585) and (512,384) under `setcam.py PID 315,185 110 0 off`, the anchor read back at `0x0081ede4`/`e8`), `key BackSpace` with the pointer on (31,18), then on (31,17) (pixel (512,451)), each followed by `cells N`; `savepark.sh` (`PLAYER=2fsfresh`) and `celltypes.py` read the types. The tool is put away after the run, so the hand is empty and Backspace is the idle lift. Ours: `ours2.py <out> <bin>` is `ours1.py` then `hover 31 18`, `backspace`, `hover 31 17`, `backspace`, comparing `cellflags rows` with `orig/run1`'s `cells-1.txt` to `cells-3.txt` at each step.
- **A coaster bought and its nodes laid in the original** (Q840, `q840/start.sh`, `q840/lib.sh`, `q840/orig/run1/`): `start.sh <outdir>` is Q843's with `q948/newsave.TPWS` copied as it is (the New Save has the Chac Atak, item 1185, researched already: its buy row is $10,000 in the rides tab at (555,281) after the Buy button (113,590); `research.py` printed `+0x10 was 1`), then `lib.sh`'s `at`/`click`/`key`/`shot`/`cells`. The load's camera shows the park's north-west corner: the pointer at (620,230) holds the station's footprint blue at cells 3..6, 6..9; the buy screen is closed at (940,430) first, and `hand.py` reads `item 1185`. **The click puts the station down** (the bank falls 10,000, 7 cells gain `0x200`, two pylon cells made), the hand then asks for the back of the queue, a chain icon at (612,305): click it once. **Nothing in the hand is the path tool** (the cursor "Left-click to build path"), and **a click on the coaster's red open end piece at (662,190) opens the coaster bar** ("Left-click to edit this track", hand vtable `0x6fe9e0`, the bar at the screen's foot; its track tool is armed already and shows the next piece's price). **A node is a click where the cursor is the pylon icon** and the preview is blue with a price; red with the no-entry cursor is refused (too near, too far or too sharp a turn: hover three or four spots with `shot` and read the pointer, the nodes were at (790,200), (810,450), (560,440) on that camera). `coaster.py PID 3 pylons` prints the coasters oldest first, so the new one is the block with `instance 463` there (not the last); `trackcells.py PID 2 X,Y ...` reads a cell's map type and parent (a pylon cell is type 21, parent the coaster's thing id). **Backspace takes the last node back** and puts its cell word back. **Escape with nothing in the hand opens the menu**, not a cancel: Resume Game is at (510,445) and Quit Game at (510,603), which asks first (the red cross at (713,326) declines). Nothing was saved: `freshfs.sh stop` removes the player and the reference `save/` read the same sum after. **Moving a pylon, and stacking one** (Q1001, `q1001/lib.sh`, `q1001/start.sh`): the load's camera needs panning first (`camto.py PID 5,8`, then the station's footprint is blue at (620,230)); the open end piece is at (700,230), its tooltip "Left-click to edit this track" (a hand cursor on (662,190) reads "view/edit this item's details" instead). `probe X Y ...` in `q1001/lib.sh` hovers each point, prints the preview cell and crops the pointer into a sheet to read (the pylon cursor or no-entry; each call overwrites the one `probe-sheet.png`, so copy it before the next; a cell that holds a pylon read no-entry each time it was hovered, and a click on one was not tried). The bar's buttons, row one then two, read: move pylons (368,610), raise or lower (425,610), swap a track section up through a stacked pylon (480,610), rotate track on top of pylons (368,670), pivot track on pylons (425,670), swap down through a stack (480,670). **The move is press, drag, hold, release, and the carry runs per frame**: click the pylon first (a press without that click since the last move carries nothing; its pixels read one hover value in `trackcarry.py`), then `xdotool mousedown 1` on it, step the pointer in four or five jumps to the target cell, wait 1.5 to 2.5 seconds with the button down, read `coaster.py PID 1 pylons` (`cell stack 2: ...` and the carried node's `stack 1`) and `groundcell.py`, then `mouseup 1`. Two clicks do nothing. A carry drawn red (no-entry cursor, a negative price) goes back to its origin cell on the release. Nothing was saved: `freshfs.sh stop` read the same sum.
- **The Large Rock Pillar bought, and the Tunnel put down, in the original** (Q843, `q843/start.sh`, `q843/lib.sh`, `q843/ours1.py`, `ours2.py`): `research.py q948/newsave.TPWS pillar.TPWS 1422` gives the New Save the pillar's buy row (it has none), `start.sh <outdir>` is `q842/start.sh` loading that file and leaving the original up, `lib.sh` the `at`/`click`/`shot`/`cells` helpers. Pillar: Buy (113,590), the features tab (845,123), the row (560,320) (Clear Land, Golden Statue, the two Huge Rocks above it; the row's check box is empty and the item is $200), the buy screen closed at (940,430), `setcam.py PID 725,265 70 0 off`, `cellfind.py PID 71,25`, a click; sold by clicking it, the bomb (230,383) and the tick (707,283). Tunnel: `setcam.py PID 185,175 115 0 off`, `PICK=1 cellfind.py PID 24,20` for the ride's pixel (881,265), its upgrades button (676,437), the Tunnel's row (380,243); **`cellfind.py` does not find a snapped add-on's cell** (its probes read 12/14 and skip 13): move the pointer by `at X Y` and read `squares.py PID 2` until it lists ten texture-0 cells (11..13,15..18), which is pixel (230,400) on that camera. **A click on a track with nothing in the hand enters the track tool (hand tool 14, the bomb cursor), and the next click demolishes the section under it**, the Tunnel with it ($400 back): look at the hand (`original/hand.py PID`) before a second click. Ours: `ours1.py <out> <bin> "1422 71 25 0"` with `CLICK=1 CAM=725,265` is ours' click route (`carry`, `hover`, `worldclick`, then a `resume` of 4.5 s before the photograph), `ours2.py <out> <bin> [X Y]` the Tunnel's put-down from `carry 1501 289`, `hover 13 17` and its sale, each with `cellflags rows` and the typed cells before and after. `original/stampdiff.py` compares two `groundcell.py` reads. The original's 0x100 band drifts while the park runs, so compare a read taken just before an act with the one just after, never against the start's. **Q844's reads** (`q844/start.sh`, `lib.sh`, `turnrun.sh ANGLE`: the rock bought and read with `modelboxes.py`, then sold): right after the pillar's buy screen closes, `cellfind.py PID 71,25` answered nothing and `hand.py` read garbage (the hand is not yet formed), so the click fell where the pointer stood (cell (76,26), anchor (74,24)); `sleep` a second and read `hand.py` for tool 4 before `cellfind.py`. **Do not click a placed pillar to look for its window while the hand holds the path tool** (tool 1): two clicks laid a path over it and its line left the listing, with no sale.
- **What a click and a drag do to a placed add-on, and the sale's sound, in the original** (Q1000, `q1000/orig/start.sh`, `lib.sh`, `q1000/ours3.py`): `start.sh <outdir>` is Q843's with its own copy of the file (the park left running, `freshfs.sh stop` after, with `OPENTPW_FULLSIM_PLAYER` set). **The sale's sound:** arm `BRK_COND='*(int*)($esp+12) >= 0x96 && *(int*)($esp+12) <= 0x99' BRK_PRINT='printf "EFFECT %d cat %d x %d\n", *(int*)($esp+12), *(int*)($esp+8), *(int*)($esp+16)' brk.sh 0x0051bfc0 1 120 40 > out &` (the argument slots are `$esp+4` 0, `+8` the category word, `+12` the effect, `+16` X, `+20` Y, `+24` Z), wait 5 s for gdb to attach, then click the thing, the bomb (230,383) and the tick (707,283). **The hover category at a cell:** `helpbar.py PID 2` polls two seconds and prints each change of category and cell; a single read can land on the hover's zeroing step. The cell is an id, (id - 1) mod 128 and (id - 1) div 128. Scan pixels with `at X Y` and `helpbar.py PID | grep -o 'cat [0-9]* cell [0-9]*'` to map a camera's pixels to cells. **Clear Land:** the Delete key arms it (a bomb cursor on the hand; the same key again, or Escape, puts it away), then two clicks, the corners from `cellfind.py PID X,Y` with no PICK (the tool's preview cell), taken with the camera set by `setcam.py` and the pointer kept off the screen's edge: **`cellfind.py`'s Newton probes reached x 27 and panned the camera** (the point went from 185 to 89 and every later pixel was a cell off; read `camera.py` after a search). A click at the gadget's corner (x < 250, y > 500) opens its screens (the Research Lab), and Escape with nothing in the hand opens the pause menu (cancel its Exit To Lobby box with the red X at (713,326), then Resume Game at (510,445)). Ours: `ours3.py <out> <bin>` puts the Tunnel down for the Dino Karts (`carry 1501 289`, `worldclick 13 17`) and prints `worldclick` on (11,16), (11,17) and (13,17) with the hand and the window beside each.
- **A row's click in the all-items list, in both games** (Q1041, `q1041/orig/start.sh`, `lib.sh`, `q1041/ours1.py`): `start.sh <outdir>` is Q1000's (the New Save loaded, the original left running; `freshfs.sh stop` after, with `OPENTPW_FULLSIM_PLAYER` and `OPENTPW_HARNESS_WORK` set). The Tunnel: `setcam.py PID 185,175 115 0 off`, the ride (881,265), Buy Upgrades (676,437), the Tunnel's row (380,243), the pointer at (230,400) and a click (Q843's). The list is the key `a` with the pointer off the park (500,600); its tabs at y 123: rides (667), sideshows (727), shops (787), miscellaneous (845); the rows 19.4 pixels apart from y 222 on the 1024 x 768 frame, the name column at x 200; a left click on a row opens its thing's window and the list goes (the green tick at (856,432) closes the window); **the New Save already holds a Tunnel and a Jump**, so a Tunnel put down makes a second row. Ours: `ours1.py <out> <bin> [probe|control]` (the venv's python, `OPENTPW_GAME_PATH` set, the desktop): `carry 1501 289`, `hover 13 17`, `worldclick 13 17`, `screen 5` for the list, `place("190")` for the shown list's rect and the row's point by the list's `rows of N units` log line (the rect and row area of each shape are at the script's top), a real `button(1)` and a photograph of each; `control` runs one click for a build that lacks the change. The console's `windows` names what is open, `unimplemented` the counted gaps. **A heading's click and the rows' order and indent** (Q1042, `q1042/ours.py <out> <bin>`, `q1042/orig/start.sh`, `drive.sh`): the original, started the same way, takes `at 500 600; key a` for the list and `at 230 185; click` for the Name heading (once descending, twice ascending), a 1024 x 768 `import` after each; ours takes `place("10")` (the list's first heading) and `button(1)`, and reads the order from the log's `Items order: tab 0` line, a child marked `id^depth`. A name's indent is measured as the first light pixel of a row's left edge on the picture (the D of DinoKarts against the D of Dino Karts Jump).
- **The queue tool's clicks and Backspace, in both games** (Q1038, `q1038/orig/lib.sh`, `q1038/ours/ours.py`, `q1038/cmp.py`): the original on the stock park: `OPENTPW_HARNESS_WORK` and `OPENTPW_XVFB` set (without `OPENTPW_XVFB` `original.sh start --offscreen` logs "Xvfb: command not found" and the load's clicks fail), `loadfile.sh load q325/stock.TPWS OUT TAG` (it can be run again in the running original for a fresh park, 40 s, **only with no tool armed**: Escape with a tool armed puts the tool away and the load list's clicks land in the park, arming the path tool), then `setcam.py PID 495,235 110 0 off` after the load has settled (3 s; a camera set earlier is overwritten) and **no `gmove.py align` after it**: its corner sweep pans the camera, and a click at a pixel then lands cells away. With that camera the pixels are `camera.py PID --aim X,Y`'s: the edit click on (50,22) is (586,441), the south run's (49,25) is (512,269), the west run's (46,25) is (318,269), the north run's (46,22) is (300,441), the east click on (49,22) is (512,441), (49,23) is (512,378). A click with the hand empty on a queue cell is the edit (tool mode 3, anchor (49,22), pending 3). `original/queuecells.py PID SECS X,Y ...` reads the tool and the cells; started 2 s before a click or a Backspace (`( ... & )`, `key BackSpace` is `xdotool keydown`/`keyup` 0.15 s apart), its lines are a trace: a cost or a refund is one step in the balance at the key (the park's own wages step it by tens a few times a minute, so read the step, not a before-and-after minutes apart). The preview cell `[0x008186b8]`, `[0x008186b4]` follows the pointer only with a tool armed (it reads 0 or stale with none, and `helpbar.py`'s cell is the picker's, not the pointer's) and snaps to the anchor's row or column, so calibrate a pixel after the edit click by moving the pointer and reading it, and click only when it is the cell. Ours: `ours.py OUT BIN A|B|C|D` (the venv's python, `OPENTPW_HARNESS_WORK` and `OPENTPW_GAME_PATH` set, on the desk), `camera 495 230 90 0`, `worldclick X Y` for each click, `backspace`, and `cell X Y`, `tool`, `money` read after each; `cmp.py ours.out ORIGDIR ours-block=orig-file ...` prints the cells that differ and the tools' modes, anchors and counts. The click counter `[0x008186dc]` and the pending list are process-wide in the original and survive a load, so a second scenario's first reading carries the first's; the cells are read from the file's own state at each load.
- **A thing bought and sold in the original, on flagged ground, and ours beside it** (Q886, `q885/orig/start.sh <outdir>`, `q885/orig/lib.sh`, `q885/ours3.py`, `ours6.py`, `ours7.py`): `start.sh` is `q780/orig/start.sh` with the file a copy of `q948/newsave.TPWS`: the original on a fresh Full Simulation player, the file loaded from its own Load list and **left running** (`OPENTPW_FULLSIM_PLAYER` and `OPENTPW_HARNESS_WORK` set, or `freshfs.sh stop` fails and leaves the original up; `freshfs.sh stop` after, which says `save/ sum the same`). Then, with `lib.sh`'s `at`, `click`, `shot`, `cells N` (a `groundcell.py` read into `cells-N.txt` and its counts): Buy (113,590), the miscellaneous tab (845,123), the list's down arrow (884,419) fifteen times, the Trash Can's row (560,397) or the Staff Room's (560,319) (`hand.py`: tool 4, item 1406 or 1411); `setcam.py PID 645,185 70 0 off` (a cell's world point is its cell x 10 + 5) then `cellfind.py PID X,Y` for the pixel and a click puts it down. A turned one: Ctrl+click (`xdotool keydown Control_L` around a 0.2 s press) on the park's own Staff Room at (42,23), `PICK=1 cellfind.py PID 42,24` for its pixel, turned 270 (`[0x0081d7a4]`). Sell: a click on the thing opens its window, the bomb (230,383 for the Trash Can's, 188,435 for the Staff Room's; look at a frame, the window moves with the thing's kind) and the tick (707,283) of the delete box. `python3 original/cellsdiff.py cells-0.txt cells-1.txt` compares reads. Ours: `ours3.py <out> <bin>` (the cases at its top; the console's `buy ITEM X Y ANGLE` takes the footprint's own anchor, which is not the original's cursor cell: the cursor on (65,19) gave (64,18) to (65,19) at turn 0, and ours' `buy 1411 64 18 0` is that; turned 270 the cursor's (65,19) gave (65,18) to (66,19), ours' `buy 1411 66 18 270`), `cellflags` and `cellflags rows` before, bought and sold, a `resume` of 4 s before the photograph (under `pause` a bought building has not risen). **Run one purchase per launch in ours**: a purchase lays an entrance path that a sale leaves, so a later footprint over that cell is refused (`will not fit ... is a path`): run each such case on a fresh launch. The save's checksum is untouched in both. **The Huge Hollow Rock** (Q888, `q888/orig/run1/`, `q888/lib.sh`, `q888/ours1.py`, `ours4.py`): `setcam.py PID 195,285 70 0 off` and `PICK=1 cellfind.py PID 21,29` find the park's own rock (the help bar reads "Huge Hollow Rock ... Hold CTRL and click to buy another"), Ctrl+click puts it in the hand (item 1427, rotation 0), `setcam.py PID 725,265 70 0 off` and `cellfind.py PID 71,25` is the cursor cell that anchors it at (69,24) (turn 0: cursor minus (2,1)), a click puts it down; sell with the window (380,330), the bomb (230,383), the tick (707,283); `original/savepark.sh NAME DEST` (`PLAYER=2fsfresh`) saves the park to read with `lib/celltypes.py`. Ours: `buy 1427 69 24 0`, `cell X Y` for a type, `resume` 4 s then `pause` before a photograph, camera `725 265 70 0`. **A turned rock** (Q842, `q842/start.sh`, `lib.sh`, `turnrun.sh ANGLE`, `ours2.py <out> <bin> "1427 X Y ANGLE"`): `start.sh` is `q888/start.sh` with its own file; then `turnrun.sh 90` does a whole case on the running original (Ctrl+click the park's rock at its pixel, `handturn.py PID 90`, `setcam.py PID 725,265 70 0 off`, `cellfind.py PID 71,25`, a real click, the live map, `savepark.sh`, `celltypes.py`, the window's bomb and tick, the live map again; its frames and reads are in `orig/run1/`) and the original stays up for the next angle (`freshfs.sh stop` after). The thing's own cell and angle are in the saved file: `lib/parkfile.py`'s `Park( path ).who( x, y )` on the anchor cell is the thing and `u16( at( thing ) + 0x10 )` its angle. Ours cannot be turned by a click (its `CarryingAngle` is set only by a move), so `buy` at the original's anchors is its side.
- **A kit's loop and the hoardings: what they stamp, in both games** (Q891, `q891/orig/run.sh`, `runk.sh`, `runf.sh`, `q891/ours.py`): `run.sh` is Q781's Splish Splash run (`q581/karts.TPWS` by `loadfile.sh`, bought on (20,20), the mouth (22,19), runs to (22,23), (16,23), (16,19)) and `runk.sh` Q768's Dino Karts run (bought on (32,25), mouth (34,25), runs to (34,29), (28,29), the preview at (28,25), the loop left open), each with `groundcell.py READ=0,0:95,84` after the load, the put-down and every run (`O-cells-N.txt`, `K-cells-N.txt`); `cellsdiff.py O-cells-0.txt O-cells-N.txt` says the stamped count (44, then 57 or 55) and `LIMIT=100` the cells. `runf.sh` is `freshfs.sh make` (needs `OPENTPW_FULLSIM_PLAYER`), a read, `PLAYER=2fsfresh savepark.sh` and a second read; `lib/stampkinds.py fresh.TPWS cells-fresh.txt` names the hoarding anchors. Ours: `q891/ours.py <out> <bin> karts|water|fresh|newsave`, each stage's `cellflags rows` (a stamped cell starred) and the newly held cells printed; the same cells as the original's at the put-down. The save's checksum is untouched.
- **Which pixels a change draws, and the same box in the original** (Q649): the frame before the change and after it at the same paused camera (an earlier item's run at the same numbers serves, `q618/runA/B-1.png`); every pixel differing by more than 30 in the sum of its channels is the change's (people and animated models aside), so a `lib/sharp.py` box wholly inside it is on what changed and one with no change is not (Q649's first two boxes, mapped by eye, were not). A box of ours maps to the original's 1024 x 768 frame by `bothcam.py`'s rule: x' = (x - 640) x 768 / 720 + 512, y' = (y - 360) x 768 / 720 + 384 (`q649/PREDICTION.txt`, P4c).
- **A gate: a run paused on a tick, to predict from its census before a turn** (Q713, `q713/confirm.py`). A resume
  then `pause` overshoots by eleven to twenty-one sweeps (the tick is read through the console every 0.4 s, and a line
  playing slows the replies), so a stop meant before a poll turn lands after it. Resume only to fifteen ticks short,
  `pause`, then `step 9` until the tick is reached (off-screen nine frames are about a sweep): the stop lands on its tick.
  At the gate print the census, then wait for a file the session writes after its prediction (`go-<tick>`). Under such
  pauses and steps GameClock's milliseconds and the sweeps drift apart (a line's 7.5 s wait took 88 sweeps), so a
  prediction that a line waiting behind another is said "by tick N" is not to be made from the original's four sweeps
  a second.

- **One frame of this game held on screen, the frame a real click is dealt in** (Q623, `q623/run4.py`): `pause` and
  `step` cannot show it, since the next frame's sweep runs before any grab, and a full-rate film misses one frame.
  `stall N` sleeps at the END of `Level.Update`, after the clicks and before the draw, so chain three: send `stall
  1500`, click by XTEST and send `stall 1500` within it (the next frame reads both, deals the click and stalls before
  drawing it), then send a third `stall 1500` during the second: the frame after reads it and stalls before ITS draw,
  so the click's own frame stands on screen for 1.5 s. Grab in the third stall's window, timed by the replies' `stall:
  N ms from wall W` lines; a grab one window earlier is the frame before the click, the control. The park clock then
  catches up the 4.5 s at once, so frames after it are not the next ones. In the original the same placement filmed
  by `record.sh` (`q623/orig/`): `setcam.py` after the buy screen's row (the align sweep pans the camera), Buy
  (117,588), the features tab (843,123), the Round Fountain's row (570,261), then the click on the ground.

- **The game's `console.log` and a harness run** (Q647). Every launch writes `console.log` and keeps the launch before as `console-prev.log`, in the game folder beside `save/` (the player's own data folder where that cannot be written; `OpenTPW.Common/ConsoleLog.cs`). A harness or test run that starts the game sets `OPENTPW_LOG_DIR` to its own run folder, or the run rotates Alexah's pair away: `parkrun.py` does it by default (`OUT`); a hand launch needs `OPENTPW_LOG_DIR=<folder>` beside `OPENTPW_GAME_PATH`. The log holds what stdout and stderr printed, so it equals the run's `run.log` with a header line (the build's commit, the start time) on top. The new file is opened first, as `console.log.new`, and `console.log` is moved over `console-prev.log` only once it is open, so a folder that cannot be written keeps both older files (a move that fails after the open leaves the run's file named `console.log.new`, said on the terminal). A launch that finds no game writes its file too, in the player's own data folder (`OpenWithoutGame`, Q798).
- **The update check, and a click on its line, with nothing leaving the machine** (Q941, `q941/confirm.py CASE [suffix]`, run with the venv's python): build a weekly-tagged copy beside the default `local` one, `dotnet build source/OpenTPW/OpenTPW.csproj -p:OpenTPWBuildTag=weekly-2026-01-01 -o ~/.cache/dn-tmp/q941/bin-weekly` (the default build is `local` and asks nothing; rebuild the solution after, the two share `obj/`), and give `parkrun.py` that folder as `BIN`. A weekly build makes its one request at the start, so `netfake.listener( "hole" )` (the 10-second timeout), `"closed"` (refused at once), `"record"` and no proxy at all (the real GitHub) each give a log line for that case alone: `Update check: asking GitHub ...` and then the answer or one warning. There is no URL override: the offer is made in the running game with the console's `update fake <weekly-tag>` (a releases reply holding that tag and this platform's zip, through the same pick), `update clear` and `update`; `control 8cfd56` is the update line (`8cfd55` the build's label) in the lobby and in either Escape menu, and reads its rectangle, text and colour. A real click is `move` and `button( 1 )` on its middle, and `netfake.browser_shim` records the link instead of opening a browser. Traps met: the real X pointer stays where the last run left it, so a colour read at rest can be the hover's (move it away first); the lobby's `windows` always lists `PlayerSlots`, so "no window" is no `GameMenu`, not the word "none"; the `control` reply says `with` and `without` the edge, and a regular expression for `with` matches both. A `git archive` of a commit unpacked with no `.git` builds `local+<that commit>` (a scratch commit from a temporary index, `git commit-tree`, needs no branch). Q941
- **A load timed in both games, and what to trust** (Q921, `q921/`): film both with wall-clock stamps and take the time from the click's release to the first frame of the loaded park (`loadtime.py`); read the game's own clock beside it (ours' `[load] ... ms in total`, the original's `tickwall.py`), which sit 0.08 to 0.17 s under the film in ours and 0.00 to 0.07 s in the original (the original's tick is one mark inside the load). Use the same file in both (`Easymode.TPWI` is byte-identical in the two installs; so is each player's `restart.INTS`). The first load of a launch is 0.2 to 0.3 s slower here than the next, so take three. Off-screen the original and this game are software-rendered (llvmpipe; lavapipe) and the desk runs this game on the real GPU (1.9 to 2.2 s against 2.0 to 2.3 s off-screen for a load); the original's has no GPU run on the desk. **A load's end is a frame, not a log line**: ours logs `Loaded jungle` 0.07 to 0.10 s before the park is drawn (the script's reader, final-ours, against the film) and the reader has 0.2 s grain. On the desk film the window's rectangle (`-i :0+X,Y` and its size), as `loadtime_ours.py` does, not the whole 2560 x 1440 display (not compared; the whole display was never filmed). Traps met: `ffmpeg -use_wallclock_as_timestamps 1` alone is lost at the encoder (frames restamped from 0); `-fps_mode passthrough -copyts` keeps them (and `loadtime.py` decodes the film with `-fps_mode passthrough` too: without it ffmpeg duplicates and drops frames to a constant rate and the frames part from the stamps, one frame late on a film with a 67 ms gap), and `ffprobe` reads them as `pts_time` (`pkt_pts_time` is empty); `loadtime.sh` run without `OPENTPW_XVFB` fails at its first `gmove.py`; another session's build and test run on the machine disturbs a timing, so ask what else ran (the Sentinel's 00:38 to 00:49 UTC window, 2026-11-03, was re-run).
- **The Load Park list's scrollbar and a file's camera, in both games** (Q918, `q918/confirm.py <run>` with the venv's python, and `q918/orig.sh cam2|scroll|hold|stop`): this game, on the desk through `parkrun.py`: `savepark S01`..`S20` (each `own_file`d), the menu's Load by real clicks, then the arrow, the track, the thumb and a held arrow by XTEST at the stream's own points (`vpt( vx, vy )` maps its 2048 x 1536 space onto the window through the list's rectangle from `control 23bce0`), each read with that `control`, which prints `list: top row N of M rows, V visible, scrollbar shown value .. thumb A to B`. **Read no census while a button is held**: a `reply` waits a second, and the hold runs long (the first run's repeat test did, 2026-11-02). The camera: `camera X Z ZOOM YAW`, `savepark`, loaded from the list: the log's `Camera: taken from the park file: ...` line. The original: `orig.sh cam2` loads a file by `loadfile.sh` with `campoi.py` polled from half a second before the click (**a read after `loadfile.sh` has run is spoiled**: its `align` sweeps the pointer to the screen's corner and the edge scroll moves the point 58 units; the first poll after the click is the file's), `orig.sh scroll` puts 20 copies of `Easymode.TPWI` in the reference player's folder, opens its list and drives it with xdotool at the 1024 x 768 picture's pixels (the stream's units over two; its rows stand in name order), `hold` holds the up arrow 1.3 s (six steps from the foot at its 30 frames a second), and `stop` removes every file made and checks `save/`.
- **A selected row removed from a scrolled list, in both games** (Q936, `q936/orig.sh start|stop`, `q936/listread.py`, `q936/confirm.py <run>` with the venv's python): the original's only list that loses a chosen row on cue is the hire list's janitors tab (the visitors list loses a guest when one leaves, which cannot be asked for). `orig.sh start` is Q919's: the pool grown by `Standard.sam` (17 to 20 rows of 12 visible), the stock park loaded; then `h`, the down arrow at (886, 440) of the 1024 x 768 frame for the top row, the pointer glided onto a row (slots at y = 230 + 19.4 x slot), `listread.py PID` for the numbers (the selection follows the pointer, so hover first), `listread.py PID expire` to drop the selected candidate, a second read and a shot. A candidate joins the pool (and the list) at random: read the count before every case. This game: `confirm.py` grows the pool as Q919's, `expire <id>` (console; takes the candidate out at once and tells the list, no sweep to top the pool up) and `control 248e`, whose reply now ends with the selected index, its row and the sprite's slot.
- **Making a list long enough to scroll, and driving its bar, in both games** (Q919, `q919/confirm.py <run>` with the venv's python, `q919/orig.sh start|visitors|hire|stop`): only the visitors list (13 rows of 10) is long in the stock park; the others are 1 to 4 rows. This game: a private folder from `gamefolder.make` with `levels/Standard.sam` changed (`StaffPoolInfo.TimeBetweenStaffUpdates 1`, `MaxNumberOfStaffPerUpdate 30`, `MaxHandymen 30`, `BeginningNumberOfHandymen 30`) holds 15 cleaner candidates after `resume`, 14 s, `pause` (the hire list's cleaners tab, `control 248e`); `hire <candidate> 49 22` eleven times (a cell can take them all) makes the all-staff cleaners tab 12 rows (`control 321`); `buy 1406 46 27 0` (Litter Bin; cells 46,27 38,27 47,33 46,26 45,27 fit) five times makes the miscellaneous items tab 13 rows (the tab `12c4bd`, then `control 190`, which since Q919 answers the shown list of the three under that id). The keys `v`, `h`, `e`, `o`, `b` open the screens; `control fffffffe` is their exit. The stock park's buy tabs hold 2 to 10 rows of 11, so the buy list's bar needs researched items to show (a Full Simulation save, Alt+R, or `original/research.py`); it was driven in Q931 (below). The bar is read from the `control` reply's `list: top row N of M rows ... thumb A to B` and clicked at the stream's rectangles (`vpt` of Q918's script). The original: `orig.sh start` copies the same keys into its `Data/levels/Standard.sam` (copy kept, md5 checked at `stop`), loads `Easymode.TPWI` with `loadfile.sh` and waits 20 s; its keys are `v` visitors, `a` all items, `s` all staff, `b` buy, `h` hire (it binds no `e` or `o`: those do nothing there); it was driven by xdotool at the 1024 x 768 picture's pixels (the stream's units over two) and its top rows read from the pictures (`orig/visitors-sheet.png`). Its hire pool grew to 20 against this game's 15.
- **The buy list's scrollbar with every item researched, in both games** (Q931, `q931/confirm.py <run>` with the venv's python, `q931/orig.sh`, `q931/mkpark.sh`, `q931/hand.py`): `researchpark.sh` makes `researched.TPWS` (12 rides, 19 features: tabs of 12 and 21 rows of 11). This game: the file copied into the private folder's jungle as the newest file (`own_file`d), `enter()` takes it, then `key("b")`, a tab by `place("1fb"|"1fd"|"1fa"|"1fc")` (rides, shops, sideshows, features), the list's census `control 1f8` (`top row N of M rows ... thumb A to B`), the row under the pointer by `footprint`'s `list selection <id>` after a pointer move to the slot (`vpt` at the list's rectangle 1009, 179, 1813, 902; slot s at y 427 + 39 s + 19, name column 1200), the hand by `hand`. The original: `loadfile.sh load researched.TPWS <outdir> <tag>`, then `orig.sh buy` (b), `orig.sh tab NAME X Y` (the features tab at 845, 123; the rides at 667, 123), `slot S TAG` (the pointer at 600, (427 + 39 S + 19) / 2 and `helpbar.py PID`'s `buy item`), `arrow down|up TAG`, `trackclick Y TAG`, `drag FROM_Y TO_Y TAG`, `rowclick S TAG` and `hand.py PID` for the item carried. The bar's picture points: down arrow (886, 419), up (886, 174), the track 191 to 401 with the thumb 34 tall. **A carry left in the hand stopped the next tab click** (no-entry cursor; seen once, on the buy screen's tab): `loadfile.sh stop` and start again, or a quick right click on the park with "RMB cancel" on, which puts a carry away (`park-engine.md`, "Abandoned, a move is a sale"; not tried in Q931). The original's three american names (Small bathroom, Super bathroom, Trash Can) sort differently from this game's, so predict the original's rows from its pictures, never this game's order.
- **The wheel over a screen, in both games** (Q930, `q930/confirm.py <run> <bin> before|after` with the venv's python, `q930/orig.sh`, `original/wheel.sh`): this game on the desk through `parkrun.py`: `camera 475 175 100` sets a known zoom, XTEST buttons 4 (up) and 5 (down) at the pointer's place are the wheel, the console's `camera` (no arguments) replies `zoom=`, and a screen is opened by the menu's Load (`control 1`) and Save (`control 2`) rows and shut by `control fffffffe`'s cross; the Load Park list wants `savepark S01`..`S20` (each `own_file`d) to scroll. One notch moves the zoom by 5 here (a camera zoom of 100 reads 105 after one notch down). The original: `loadfile.sh load` the stock `easymode.TPWI`, then `orig.sh z` (zoom, from `camera.py`), `orig.sh w UP|DOWN X Y N` (a pointer glide, then `wheel.sh` N times: the X wheel buttons never reach the original, so xdotool's `click 4` and `5` are not the way), `orig.sh click X Y` and `orig.sh key Escape` at the 1024 x 768 picture's pixels (Escape opens the menu: Load (510, 50), Save (510, 125), Restart Park (510, 205), Options (510, 365); the Load or Save cross (856, 430); the gadget's map button (72, 697) and the map's tick (990, 690); the Restart box's cross (713, 326)). The Load Park list holds only what is in the reference player's `jungle` folder: 20 copies of `easymode.TPWI` named `S01.TPWS`.. make it scroll, removed with `rm S[0-9][0-9].TPWS` before `loadfile.sh stop`.
- **F1 and the wheel over a park screen, here** (Q870, `q870/run1.py`, off-screen): `enter()`, `camera 475 175 100` before each wheel, XTEST buttons 4 and 5 (Q930's `wheel()`), the console's `camera` for `zoom=`; the key `b` opens the buy screen (its open logs `Park advisor: post message=188`), `place("1ed")` is its stats panel (body, not the list) and `place("1e9")` its root for a point beside it; `key("F1")` and the log's `Park advisor: post` lines counted before and after; `windows` says the screen still stands. The original's side needs the advisor's kept record at `+0x16d0` and `[0x007909ec]` read from memory (Q231).
- **A list's selection as it opens, under the pointer and under the cursor keys, here** (Q871, `q871/run2.py`,
  off-screen): `enter()`, `pause`, the pointer put on the bare park, `key("v")` opens the visitors list; the console's
  `control 1e497` ends `selected index N (row R), sprite on slot S`, and a pick under the pointer logs `List 0x1e497: row
  R at N picked under the pointer`. The visitors rows are **56 units** a row from the row area's top 315 (`Visitors list:
  rows of 56 units`), not Q117's 44: a point made with 44 lands a row high (run1). Row n's middle is the list's
  `place("1e497")` rectangle scaled from (272, 181, 1817, 930) at row-area x (294 + 1720) / 2, y 315 + 56n + 28.
  Escape shuts the screen and V opens it again with the pointer left at rest; `key("Down")`, `key("Home")` are the
  cursor keys.
- **The update notice and a ride's window, photographed on the desk, and a person's puffs counted by cell** (Q945; `q945/photos.py fake`, `q945/ours2.py <out> <bin> <secs>`, both run with the venv's python): `photos.py` is `q941/confirm.py`'s `fake` case with `openthing 13` and a frame of the Belly Bounce's window added at the end, so one launch gives the lobby, the lobby's Escape menu, the park's Escape menu (each with the gold "Update available" above the label) and the window; its three teal checks (P6, P8, P9) read `NOT as predicted` since the notice went gold (`(6, 236, 162, 4)`), which is the old script, not a fault. `ours2.py` plays the stock park on the desk, the camera on the Belly Bounce, and groups the log's `Person puff:` lines by the cell they stood on; the riders' counts to set beside them are the log's `Person N been AdmitPerson'd to ride 13` (taken on) and `Person N: successfully left ride 13` (let off). A bus load's arrival cell is (53, 5) for the small vehicle and (53, 3) for a larger one (`ParkPeople.ArrivalCell`), so a run's arrival puffs stand at one or the other. The arrival puff stands under the bus shelter's glass roof and was not photographed from above or from behind the bus.

- **The daily fork watch** (2026-10-16, `fork-review-2/watch/2026-10-16/`): what the meneerkrabs fork newly reads from
  the PC executable since the last look, and what upstream (github.com/OpenTPW/OpenTPW, every branch, from 2026-10-19)
  newly does, handed to Alexah as a report. (0) `upstreamwatch.py` (read-only clone `~/.cache/tpw-fork-upstream`, tips per
  branch in `upstream-state.json`, `--keep upstream-watch-<date>.json` once handed on) says which upstream branches moved; a moved branch's readers
  get a sweep and a verify as below from `upstream-lobby/sweep-brief.md`, and a move of upstream/main is the merge task,
  reported, never merged from the watch. (0b) `forkscan.py` (Alexah, 2026-10-25: every fork of OpenTPW, not one) names
  the other forks' new and moved branches; each `WATCH` branch gets `forkwatch.py --fork OWNER/REPO@BRANCH` for its
  executable leads (dumped and swept with the rest) and a sweep of what its readers and docs know about the data files
  (`watch/2026-10-25-forks/BRIEF.md` is the model), a verify as below, then `--keep` on both. (1) `forkwatch.py` writes `watch-<sha>.json`.
  Each script's `--keep` takes the file of the look handed on and records that look's tips, fetching nothing, so a
  fork that moves after the look is seen by the next one. (2) Make `funcs.json` by hand, a list of the leads' `func` keys (no script writes it;
  `watch/2026-10-16/dump.py` reads it), run a dump script like that one through the private Ghidra
  (`ghidra-private/gd`, never the `ghidra` MCP server while a session holds it) to write `decomp.json`, then
  `mkwatchev.py watch-<sha>.json <dir>`. (3) One sweep agent per area (`tpw-sweep`, Sonnet) reads
  `claims-<area>-N.txt` and writes `sweep-<area>.md` from a `BRIEF.md` (who we are, the fork, its Mac and Theme Park
  Inc traps: copy the last day's). (4) One `tpw-verify` agent per area settles every high and medium lead and every
  conflict from `objdump` of `testme.exe` and at most fifteen private `gd` calls, reading our facts from committed
  `main` by `git grep` (`VERIFY.md` is its brief), and writes `verify-<area>.md`; only CONFIRMED, or the confirmed
  part of PARTLY, is used. (5) Write `REPORT.md` (leads that held and what they serve, our doc lines found wrong) and
  report to Alexah. (6) **File only on Alexah's word** (theirs, 2026-10-16, in the coordinator session: "file and fix"):
  notes under the items the leads serve, new items after the queue's last, our wrong doc lines fixed, each citing
  "fork watch <date>" and the verify file's lead id. Never a sweep-only claim.
- **A member's walk watched in the original, and a patrol area set without the tool** (Q434, `q434/orig/run-b.sh`):
  the person's own `+0x8`/`+0xc` are not its place. The navigator is at `+0xd4`, so `thingwords.py ... 26:0xdc:I:px
  26:0xe0:I:py` (16.16: the cell is the value `>> 16`), `26:0x18:H 26:0x1a:H` the destination (cell x 256 + 128 at a
  cell's centre), `26:0x11c:I` the stamp of the last route planned (it moves on every successful roll), `26:0x30:I` the
  thought and `26:0x19c:I` the state. The patrol tool's click writes `+0x20a`/`+0x20c` and nothing else
  (`FUN_00506e50`), so `thingpoke.py %PID% 0.5 4 26:0x20a:H=3380 26:0x20c:H=3511` (kind 4) fences a member exactly,
  where Q433's clicks P1/P2 land on other cells as the camera follows Mike between runs.
- **A build tool's money refusal in both games** (Q542, `q542/orig/run2.sh`, `q542/confirm.py`): a park with a low
  balance is `parkfile.economy_set( stock, dst, dict( balance = 50 ) )`, but in the original the gate's takings lift it
  by about 125 before the first read after `loadfile.sh`, so hold it: the bank is the thing at world `+0x1da726` (8,
  kind 16), `thingpoke.py %PID% 120 16 8:0xc:i=50`, and `bank.py` reads it. The tools' cursor is the short at
  `[0x007cb2d8]` (8 `c_noplace`, 20 `c_cash`), `d5c` the money flag, `[0x0081af4c]` the run's total, read with
  `tpwmem.py watch 0x0081af4c:i 3` (it reads 0 between resets, so take the greatest). Here `load()` pauses, so the
  file's balance stands; `strip X Y` prints `run total N` (the second charges in it) and marks a square refused for money `$`, and `pointer` the
  cursor. **Our Load Park list is in name order** (`SaveFolder.SavedParks`): with two files copied in, row 1 is the first
  by name, not the newest, so take the first out before loading the second.
- **The patrol tool in either game** (Q433, `q433/orig/run.sh` and `run2.sh`, `q433/confirm.py`): on the original's
  1024 x 768 frame the staff window's patrol switch is (739,385); with the tool armed `[0x008186b8]`, `[0x008186b4]` is
  the preview's cell, so moving the pointer over a grid and reading them maps the view (`run2.sh`). The original's
  camera moves with Mike between runs, so read the cells in the same run as the clicks. **Its Left key turned the view
  and Down drew it back; neither panned**: the pointer resting on a screen edge pans, about 8 cells a second;
  `KEYS="Left:5" EDGE="1022 300 1.2;512 766 1.0"` brought the band outside the park (x 42..53, y 14..16) into view.
  Here `camera X Y 60 0` (10 units a cell) and `lib/cellprobe.py` find the cells; the console's `strip` is the outline
  and `pointer` its cursor. **Set `camera` after the switch is down**: the strip's locator follows Mike, so a camera set
  before Ctrl+S is lost; at `camera 475 190 90 0` cellprobe finds (43,16), outside the park, and (43,21), whose rectangle
  from (43,16) is blue, so one anchor shows a red pass then a blue outline (Q534, `q534/confirm.py`). **An anchored one-cell rectangle is four squares** on that cell (each line lays it):
  measured here, read in the original's code. A run that waits longer than a line (0xc1, 18 s) before the click that
  puts the tool away shows no cut. **Here `0xc1` leaves its slot a tick after it is posted**, as the advisor starts
  saying it: read `advisor`'s `message=193/.../said=1`, not `slot=193/` (Q437's first miss); a way out's cut is the
  log's `withdraw message=193 ... cut=1` just before its own line (`q437/confirm.py`). **A quick right click** (Q438): in
  the original `xdotool mousedown 3`, 0.08 s, `mouseup 3` on `:77` (under the 200 ms limit); here `button(3, ...,
  held=0.06)`. Opening Mike's window again snaps the original's camera to wherever he has walked, so aim that phase's
  cells at the camera's own point (`q438/orig/run.sh`, `AIMREL=1`). A right press of any length lands, one let go inside a slow frame too (Q499; `button(3, what, held=0)` is
  the original's one call `xdotool mousedown 3 mouseup 3`); with the hand full by `carry 1100` and `rmbcancel on`, a
  put-away is the log's `world click: right click - drop:` and `hand` reading `item 0` (`q499/confirm.py`, which also
  leaves first person by it; the original's `q499/orig/run.sh` reads first person by `helpbar.py`'s flags). Each
  release logs `right release: armed=<0|1> held=<ms> ms`. **A stopped frame** (Q533): here the console's `stall <ms>`
  stops the frame once the park has had its buttons and tick (`DebugConsole.StallFrame`, the end of `Level.Update`); send
  it 50 ms after an XTEST press and the release 300 ms later (`q533/confirm.py`). In the original, `kill -STOP` the
  game's pid, the release, then `kill -CONT` (`q533/orig/run.sh`): where the stop falls in its frame is not chosen, so
  count rounds (3 of 5 put away). **A refused click in the original** (Q439,
  `q439/orig/run.sh` with `AIMB=1`): `camto.py %PID% 48,18`, then `outside.py` for the nearest band of outside cells and
  `cellfind.py` for each corner, read straight before its click. Its sound under the music scored 0.32 only: switch the music off first (`q439/PREDICTION.txt`). With it off
  (Q442, `q442/orig/run.sh`): `setcam.py PID 435,165 110 0 off` draws (43,16)'s middle at the frame's middle (512,384),
  and a press there with the hand empty arms the path tool red (mode 1, `d48` 1, read with the button held); its
  release refuses, and `original/capmatch.py` found `blnl_error1` at 0.558 there, the capture's only window over 0.3.
  With nothing armed after the menu the preview's cell reads (0, 0), so
  `cellfind.py` finds nothing there: aim with the camera instead. **A laying click's sound in the original** (Q632,
  `q632/orig/run.sh`, `BRK=1`): the same aim, `setcam.py` moved to the second cell between the clicks. A 123 ms ui clip
  scores only 0.29 to 0.35 in the mix (0.16 to 0.19 quiet), and a look-alike sample nearly as high, so name the effect
  by `brk.sh 0x0051bfc0` with `BRK_COND='*(int*)($esp+12) == 0x65'`: one hit a click, the first stack word the caller's
  return. `oneshots.py` does not see a ui effect at all (bit `0x1`, below). The queue tool there (Q671, `q671/orig/run.sh`):
  `setcam.py` over the Belly Bounce's queue cell (51,22) and a click at the frame's middle hands it over (mode 3, anchor
  (49,22)), then `setcam.py` over (47,22) and a click joins the path; here `q671/confirm.py` clicks the same cells. **The refusal latch a tool leaves** (Q532,
  `q532/orig/run.sh`, `q532/confirm.py`): in the original, `setcam.py` to (49,24), click (the path tool armed and
  anchored), `setcam.py` to (51,24), click (refused on the Belly Bounce), then Ctrl+S and the mechanics' button, read
  `[0x00816d50]` and `squares.py`; here `cellprobe.py`'s `find` arms `tool path`, and each probe's pass moves the latch,
  so probe a cell outside the park last (`tool` then reads `latch 0`), and find each cell at the camera it is clicked
  at: (51,24) and (49,24) at `camera 495 230 90 0`, (43,16) at `camera 475 190 90 0`, not the other way.
  **The queue tool in either game** (Q538, `q538/orig/run.sh`, `q538/confirm.py`): a click on the Belly Bounce's queue
  cell (50,22) with the hand empty edits its queue, mode 3 anchored on its last cell (49,22) (here `tool` says so; there
  `[0x0081ae2c]` 3 and the anchor `[0x0081ede4]`, `[0x0081ede8]`), and the pass runs every tick over the pointer's cell,
  so a hover alone sets the latch: (50,22) the queue's middle, (48,22) the join, (49,17) type 7 land. Type 7 land there
  is NOT outside the park (flags 0: `cell 49 17`); `parkfile.py -n` lists where a NOMODIFY path meets land that is
  ((48,17) and (48,16), flags `0x4c0`). Here (50,22) and (48,22) are found at `camera 495 225 90 0`, (49,17) and (48,17) at
  `camera 485 175 90 0`: set the click's camera back before the click. A console scene (`tool queue 13`, a click to
  anchor, `hover X Y`) must be ended with Escape while `hover` still holds the cell: released first, the pass runs over
  the pointer's own cell and moves the latch.
  **A queue edited, lengthened and taken back, here** (Q860, `q860-b18/confirm.py`, off-screen, stock park paused): `worldclick 50 22` edits
  the Belly Bounce's queue (`tool` then reads mode 3, anchored (49,22), 3 pending: the walk's list), `worldclick 49 25` and
  `worldclick 46 25` lay a run south and one west (`cell X Y` reads `overlap`, the counter: (49,22) and (49,25) 1, the cells laid fresh 0;
  `money` the balance, 75 a cell), and `backspace` takes the last run up (`took up the queue run from (49,25) to (46,25)`), leaving
  (49,25) at 0. `camera 495 230 90 0` frames it. Not run in the original.
  **The locator's edges over a tool's strip** (Q540, `q540/orig/` with `lib.sh`, `q540/confirm.py`): commit a fence
  for Mike Cooper over the Belly Bounce (his window, the switch up then down, (52,26) anchored, a click on (54,27)), edit
  the queue, then Ctrl+S and the mechanics' button: the queue tool stays armed in both games and the edges are drawn
  over its strip. In the original they are drawn only while the follow runs (`setcam.py PID off` takes them away, `on`
  brings them back, the pointer kept); the follow holds the zoom, and the pick does not reach through the locator's
  panel, so (48,22) cannot be pointed at there: use the join (49,28), north of the anchor. Here `strip_end_at` finds it
  under the follow. In the original's mode 0 the preview's cell does not move: find cells with a tool armed.

- **The staff window's FIRE and PICK UP in either game** (Q432, `q432/orig/run.sh`, `q432/confirm.py`): on the
  original's 1024 x 768 frame `b_fire` is (228,385) and `b_pick` (308,385); FIRE's box puts its tick at (710,282) and
  its cross at (714,326); here they are `control 50c`, `50e`, `9873c9` and `9873c8`. Fire the stock park's one mechanic
  last: with him gone the mechanics' button opens a locator with nobody, and the strip opens no window. The gadget's
  money readout moves with income every second, so a cost is read by `original/bank.py` either side of the click, and
  here by `money`'s `staff` total. The strip's click folds the locator and ends the follow, so the camera stays put while
  the member walks on with the window up: a frame from before the strip's click is not the view at the pickup. Shoot
  the window, the carry and the drop, which share a camera.
- **A picked-up member's cell, photographed in either game** (Q1032, `q1032/confirm.py`; the original `q1032/orig/lib.sh` and `seq5.sh`): the staff window covers
  the middle of the view, so the cell is photographed with the camera moved off it. Ours (`confirm.py <out> <bin>`, desk, `parkrun.py`): the stock park, wait for
  tick 1040; Ctrl+S, `control 95f` (the mechanics' button), `control 146` (the strip), the pointer to the far corner (width - 40, 40) after every click; `pause`; then
  rounds of the console's `camera X Y` (direct, not eased): probe +30 in X and +30 in Y, read `circle 26`'s `origin pixel` each time, solve the 2 x 2 step that puts
  it at (1100,300) (right of the window, above the advisor's picture) and move; stop within 6 px (three rounds in run2 and run3). Shots A and A2 (0 px apart), PICK UP
  (`control 50e`), the pointer back to the corner, `hand` says worker 26 and `circle 26` "not drawn", shot B; diff the crop (ox-60, oy-80, ox+60, oy+40). A person's
  shadow is its own quad, so the diff runs past the sprite's rectangle. The original (off-screen :77; `lib.sh` gives `at`, `click`, `ctrl`, `shot`, `rd`, `corner`):
  `loadfile.sh load q325/stock.TPWS`; the window by `seq5.sh`'s `window()` (Ctrl+S, the mechanics' button (360,613), the strip (380,505), `corner` = the pointer to
  (880,650) between clicks; Q431's clicks below); read Mike's address from `[0x007c2658]` with the window up (seq5.sh hard-codes one process's); let him walk, then start
  `memhold.py PID 120 0x007859a0:I=1` by hand (a member put down under the hold is not drawn until the clock runs; the sequence takes about 40 s); then close the window
  by its OK (816,378) and open it again so the strip's snap puts the camera on him (`camera.py PID` prints the camera's point, his place at the snap, not his cell's
  centre), OK, shots A and A2, the window again, PICK UP (308,385), the pointer back, shots B and B2; his state `+0x19c` reads 7 held. **Park the pointer at
  (880,650)**: past 0.95 of the way to an edge pans the camera. `pkill -f memhold` kills the shell that names it; kill by `pgrep -f '^python3 .*memhold'`, and the hold
  ends on its SECS by itself, writing the old value back.

- **The shop's window in either game** (Q31, `q31/orig/run.sh`, `run2.sh`, `q31/confirm.py`): the stock park at camera

  clock (NOT TRUE) turns a day and sells a drink within the run: read its figures from the opening frame.
- **The staff member's window in either game** (Q431, `q431/orig/run.sh`, `q431/confirm.py`): Ctrl+S, the mechanics'

- **A member put down under the pointer, here** (Q869, `q869/run1.py`, `run2.py`, off-screen): `enter()`, `pause`,

  thing N back down at (x,y)". In the file (`pk.Park`, `p.at( N )`) a staff record's `mX` and `mY` are the u16s at `+8`

  and thing 1's `mStaffMemberPickedUp` the u16 at `+20` (FileFormats `saves.md`). A second save in the same run takes

- **The pointer over a window's model preview in either game** (Q634, `q634/confirm.py`, `q634/orig/run.sh`): here the

- **A carried thing's squares in both games** (Q633, `q633/orig/run3.sh`, `q633/confirm.py`): the stock park at camera

  `cellprobe.py`'s `find` under `tool path` first (with a thing in the hand the drawn census starts at the centred anchor,

- **A message box in either game, and its cross** (Q410, `q410/confirm.py`, `q410/orig/run.sh`): here the console's

- **The hire screen's account in both games, and a month turned** (Q385): this game's `money` prints the analyser's
  money months (`month balance N slot S cashin T/N staff T/N costs T/N slot S ...`, the month so far over the newest
  closed; the balance ring has no month so far, Q387); the log's
  `Hire screen: opened, ...` and `Hire screen: candidate ...` lines and the training screen's `account a / b / c / d`
  are the figures. The park sweeps four times a second, so the stock park's first month here (near tick 1470) is
  about three minutes after the pause is lifted: wait for `Park: analyser month closed` up to eight minutes. **The
  gadget's Buy button reopens the screen it was left on**: after the hire screen it opens the hire screen itself, and

  7ca2f8:i 7ca300:i` (cash in, staff, all costs, other) and its rings `thingwords.py ... 5:0x1fc90:i 5:0x1fed4:i
  5:0x1f7f0:i 5:0x1f5a0:i 5:0x1f7e4:i` (`q385/orig/run.sh`). **A file this game saves after its own first month is past
  the original's month too**: the original closes a month on the load's first tick, so its screen shows what the file
  held as the month so far.

- **Does the original's draw use this at all** (Q361): before poking a texture's or a material's flags to see what
  changes, read-watch the word (`WATCH=rwatch watch.sh ADDR 4 4 15`) beside a control that is read (`mGameTick`, or a
  texture of the same model that is drawn). A poke of a word nothing reads changes nothing and proves nothing; the
  floors' did, two rounds running. Find the word by its pointers: `texcache.py` for the texture record, `pointsat.py`
  up to its material slot, material object and source record (`floormats.py` does all three).

- **A sign's board beside the original's** (Q357): Lost Kingdom's park gate, from inside the park. This game:
  `camera 480 140 80 180` and `step 2` under `pause`, saved; the original given that file through
  `original/screenpark.sh` frames the board alike since Q441 (one lens: "Cameras in both games"); any difference left is the script's own framing (`screenpark.sh` frames the camera's own point, Q359). Re-check `q357/measure.py`'s patches, which were placed for the two scales, before reusing it.
  `q357/measure.py <frame> orig|ours` reads two lettering-free patches of its face and the slab's top. The lobby's
  boards are no use: in the original's orbit the player buttons cover them, and this game's `island N` frames
  them small and mid-flight.

**In OpenTPW, through the console** (`OPENTPW_DEBUG_CONSOLE=1`; the `case` labels in `DebugConsole.cs` are the
whole list):

- **A fresh park's first days of weather** (Q803, `q803/confirm.py <out> <bin> fresh|stock`, off-screen): enter, pause (sweep 8), read `weather`'s "as saved"
  and `flags`, resume to sweep 84, then `step 6` a half sweep at a time past the delivery; `final1` is the shape of a run. The weather thing's record against the original's: `q803/w7.py <mine> <ref>`.
- **A fresh Full Simulation park, in both games** (Q349). This game: `lib/freshpark.py` (a player folder with no
  `gms.dat` is a new Full Simulation player); the park's first log line is `jungle: fresh park: ...`, the poll
  starts on tick 1, so pause at once (tick 8 on the desktop and off it), and row N's first turn is its place
  among the polled rows (row 80: tick 50). The original: a folder in the reference install's `save/users/` under
  a free slot digit, holding a Full Simulation player's `gms.dat` and empty theme folders; two clicks on its row
  of the player list (660, 158 for the second row) go straight into Lost Kingdom, which it makes fresh and
  writes as `jungle/restart.INTS` within the minute. Remove the folder afterwards (`original/freshfs.sh`).
  **A Full Simulation scene both games load** (Q327): in the original's fresh park, off-screen, its buy screen (Buy
  (117,588), the misc tab (843,123), a row at y 261 + 19.5k, then the ground: one feature a row, the hand empty after
  each) and its hire screen (the side tab (957,316), the researchers' tab (843,121), a candidate's row, then the
  ground) build the scene; Escape, Save (510,128), a name typed with Shift held for capitals, Return writes it to
  `2fsfresh/jungle` (copy it out before `freshfs.sh stop`). The file, edited if need be (`parkfile.advisor_forget`;
  `mParkClosed` through `pk.Park`'s `hdr['closed']`), is loaded in the original by `original/fsload.sh` and here
  with `lib/freshpark.py`'s folder holding it in `JUNGLE` before `enter()`, then `load(1)` (`q327/confirm-load.py`).
  The original's sweep ran about 6.5 a second and this game's 4.8: compare turns and order, not a line's length in sweeps.
- **Is a float product rounded before its compare** (Q343): break on the `FCOMPP` with `BRK_PRINT` (`brk.sh`'s
  row) as the `POLL` of a `loadfile.sh` load: `fctrl` 0x27f is 53-bit precision, so `FILD`, `FMUL float ptr` is a
  double of the float key (five times the float nearest 0.2 is more than one). The same hit gives the row's turn
  (its tick) and both sides of the compare.
- **The x87 control word where a routine of the sweep rounds** (Q1040, `q1040/orig/run.sh a`): the stock park (`q325/stock.TPWS`) by
  `loadfile.sh`, its `POLL` one line, `BRK_PRINT='printf "fctrl %x\n", $fctrl' brk.sh 0x004df0ae 8 300 8 > OUT &`; eight hits of
  `FUN_004dedf0` (a guest put down or sent to stand) came by tick 952, about 150 ticks after the load, both callers among them.
- **A polled row whose shipped score is under the advisor's 25** (Q344, row 79's `Score` 20): neither game can
  say it as shipped, so both are run under a raised `Score` beside the key whose edge is asked (`q344/confirm.py`,
  `q344/orig/run.sh` with `POORER=`, `MORE=`, `SCORE=`); `Score` 25 is the control that the limit is strict
  (`result=under-threshold` here, a fresh record there). A whole-number share's edge is one key apart: 62 silent
  and 63 said pins five kinds of eight in both games. **The leavers' summons in this game is not on a fixed tick**
  (1093 in Q341's runs, 1139 in Q344's): a day-29 check asks whether a stamp came before the row's next turn.
- **A polled row whose first sayable turn is late** (Q345, row 81 on tick 1593): one scene run saves the park
  at each tick wanted (`q345/scene.py`: `save()` then a copy, as often as needed; a purchase and a sale between
  saves make the controls), so four files cost one run. **A spent bus is summoned again on a fixed tick of the
  stock park (1264, 1438, 1600 here) and both games may draw a ferry or seaplane there, which stamps the newest
  build**: read the scene run's `summoned` lines and save between two of them, never just before one that falls
  on the row's turn. A day-short file is seen said a turn later only in the original with its stamp held
  (`EXTRA="python3 -u .../thingpoke.py %PID% 150 13 5:0x21c30:Q=<stamp>"`). **A tag lives thirty seconds**: after
  `loadfile.sh` returns (some 20 s after the click) a `run.sh` that takes frames first clicks a tag that is
  ending; `WAIT60=0 SECS60=0 CLICKS=1` clicks in time, and `GRAB=1` has the frames of him speaking.
- **Four kinds of ride in an Instant Action park** (Q343): `stockscene.py ... 912 "buy 1101 41 22 0" "buy 1102 70
  40 0" "buy 1104 55 30 0"` (a Crazy Ape, a Rocky Racers and an Aztec Mayhem beside the stock Belly Bounce, each
  left shut as bought, which both games count as a kind standing). The Instant Action catalogue holds twelve kinds; four are on offer at the start and the lab can bring the rest. Made at
  765 (paused on 768) with `fire 30` first, the stock researcher dismissed, the file keeps the lab's eight ride
  projects and both games say the advisor's row 16 on tick 783 (Q421, `q421/all4.TPWS`).
- **A member of staff's grade written in a file, and the second tag's click** (Q350): `parkfile.thing_set( src,
  dst, 30, 398, '<i', grade )` writes the stock researcher's `mCurrentPayGrade`, which the original reads back at
  his `+0x1e4` (`thingwords.py ... 30:0x1e4:I`). **A stock park whose researcher is of grade 0 hears a training
  line on tick 1109 in the original**, so a line said after it waits some twenty ticks and its tag is the second
  on the mast: click it at game point (59, 433), not the first tag's (59, 471). A run that ends with a box or a
  screen open (the bar's research button leaves one) breaks the next `loadfile.sh` load: make it the chain's last.
- **The original's Buy Upgrades and a track add-on put down** (Q780, `q780/orig/start.sh <outdir>`: `fsload.sh`'s load of
  Alexah's played karts park left running): `setcam.py PID 185,175 115 0 off`, a click on the ride ((24,20) by `PICK=1
  cellfind.py`, from `512,384` with the camera on the cell) opens its window; its upgrades button is (676,437), the list's rows
  (380,224) the Jump and (380,243) the Tunnel (1024 x 768). With the add-on in the hand `cellfind.py` chases the snapped
  preview cell, and probes near an edge pan the camera: map the pixels with no tool in hand, camera set on the cell, then buy
  and `gmove.py` there. `squares.py` reads the squares, `trackcells.py` the cells (map record too), and the new thing, which may
  take a freed id (the Tunnel took 209), is found by walking the thing table for its item; `savepark.sh` with `PLAYER=2fsfresh` gives
  the file. `freshfs.sh stop` after. In ours `carry <item> 289`, `hover`, `step 90` (the squares glide to the pointer, which
  `pause` holds), `strip`, `worldclick` (`q780/ours.py`, `pics.py`).
- **The original's research screen in Full Simulation on the real screen** (Q705, `q705b/orig/drive.sh OUTDIR`): a
  fresh Full Simulation player made as `freshfs.sh make` makes one (Alexah's `gms.dat` in `save/users/2fsfresh`),
